# Web UI Polish & Glitch Audit — post-0.22 (Afterglow)

**Date:** 2026-07-25 · **Build audited:** `0.22.0` on `feat/0.22`, live server (`visual` testing profile, port 3338)
**Status:** findings only — no code changed.

## Method

- 23 surfaces captured full-page from a live seeded server at 1440x900 and 768x1024, in **both** themes (92 screenshots), plus all 10 settings tabs.
- 17 audit agents (9 per-surface, 8 cross-cutting lenses: typography, colour/depth, design-system utilization, dialogs, states, glitches, responsive/a11y, product feel) read the screenshots **and** the templates, `app.css`, `design-system.css`, the Stimulus controllers, and `dev/design-system/DESIGN.md`.
- Every finding was then put through an adversarial verifier that had to refute it against the named screenshot and file. **19 findings were killed** (unsupported causal claims, sanctioned-by-canon behaviour, mis-cited evidence, already-scheduled 0.24/0.25 feature work misfiled as polish).
- Console/network sweep across 16 surfaces: **zero JS errors, zero failed requests.** The problems are visual and structural, not runtime.

## Result: 232 confirmed findings

| Severity | n | | Scope | n |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| High | 69 | | Canon change | 40 |
| Medium | 135 | | Glitch fix | 72 |
| Low | 28 | | Adoption gap | 118 |
| **Total** | **232** | | Feature | 2 |

### By category

| Category | n |
|---|---|
| component-adoption | 41 |
| typography | 33 |
| state-coverage | 31 |
| layout-spacing | 31 |
| color-depth | 27 |
| interaction-glitch | 20 |
| accessibility | 13 |
| dialog-feedback | 13 |
| responsive | 10 |
| copy-content | 8 |
| brand | 5 |

---

## What 0.22 did and did not do

0.22's own structural acceptance criteria genuinely held — verified: **zero** inline `style=` attributes in templates (ceiling was 5), **zero** template-local `<style>` blocks, 215 of 220 `font-size` declarations in `app.css` use tokens. The milestone delivered what it promised.

What it promised was *compliance*, not *craft*. Its PRD lists "new UX features" as an explicit non-goal and its success criterion was a page-by-page compliance table reading "good". A page can be fully compliant with a design system whose own type scale, surface ladder and component set are thin — and that is what the audit found.

---

## The five root causes

Most of the 232 findings are downstream of five defects **in the canon itself**. Fix these and a large fraction of the list collapses.

### 1. The surface ladder is collapsed — this is literally "flat and gray"

Card-versus-ground luminance separation measures **1.02:1 to 1.10:1 — below the just-noticeable-difference threshold**. The body gradient (`components.css:56`) runs `crust → base → mantle`, and `--bg-mantle` is *also* the card fill token, so on part of every page a card is exactly the colour of the ground it sits on. In dark theme cards are in places *darker* than the ground, so elevated surfaces read as holes. Light theme is worse: sidebar, topbar and cards all resolve to the same token, so the shell has no chrome-versus-content separation at all.

### 2. Colour is entirely hover-gated

**0.50%–1.45% of content pixels carry any chroma at rest.** The `.card-metric--*` and `.card-tint-*` variants put all of their hue in `:hover`, so a stationary page is uniformly neutral. That is the "gray" complaint measured.

### 3. The type scale wastes 3 of its 7 steps inside a 2px band

`--text-xs` 12px, `--text-sm` 13px, `--text-base` 14px are three tiers within 2px of each other, and they absorb **~90% of all font-size declarations** (103 + 79 out of 220 in `app.css` alone are the two smallest). Compounding it: DESIGN.md names **8 typographic tiers** (caption, body-sm, body-md, label-md, heading-md, page-title, display, metric-value) but the CSS ships **only raw size tokens plus two utilities** — so every consumer re-derives size + weight + line-height + tracking by hand, and reliably picks the smallest. There is no `.t-heading` / `.t-metric` composite class to apply.

### 4. Canon has no form primitives, no tab component, and no dialog primitive

- **Forms:** zero input/label/checkbox/radio/toggle rules in `components.css`, while templates use a bespoke form family 250+ times. DESIGN.md documents a "Native selects" section with *no backing CSS*. Settings — the app's densest surface — therefore invented its own flat controls, and two divergent tab bars.
- **Dialogs:** no `.dialog` frame, backdrop, or action-row recipe exists at all, so the app privately invented one (`.task-dialog`). And DESIGN.md's feedback decision table **actively sanctions `confirm()`** — the single upstream blocker behind every native-dialog defect.

### 5. One 900px container for prose and data alike

`--container-max: 900px` is applied to reading surfaces and data-dense surfaces identically. On a 1440px viewport that leaves ~280px of empty gutter while tables are crushed — **this is the direct cause of the `PROVID/ER`, `CREATE/D`, `TO/KE/NS` mid-word header wrapping** on the Tasks page. There is no wide-container tier.

---

## Worked example: the Health dashboard

The clearest single illustration. `health_dashboard.html` uses **neither `.metric-value` nor `.meter`** — verified by grep; five other templates (`workflow_detail`, `settings`, `memory_dashboard`, `workflow_step_detail`, `components`) do use them. The app's actual dashboard renders every KPI — uptime, DB size, session count, FTS5 status — as 13px label/value text rows, at the same size and weight as their own labels. No metric tier, no meters, no chart ramp, one orphaned card in a two-column grid. The primitives exist, are served, and were skipped on the one page that most needs them.

---

## A note on runtime CDN dependencies (found during capture, outside the agents' scope)

`layout.html` loads three assets from external CDNs at every page load:

| Asset | Source | Failure mode when unreachable |
|---|---|---|
| JetBrains Mono | `fonts.googleapis.com` | The design system's **single deliberate typeface** silently falls back to system monospace — every metric of the type system changes |
| htmx 2.0.8 | `unpkg.com` | The entire fragment-swap interaction model stops working |
| marked 15 | `cdn.jsdelivr.net` | Chat messages render as raw markdown |

highlight.js, DOMPurify, Stimulus and `sse.js` **are** vendored locally under `static/`. The split is arbitrary and undocumented. This also contradicts ADR-047's stated promise that a bare binary is "fully self-sufficient — single-file install, no network dependency", and it sends every user's IP to Google on every page load from a self-hosted, security-conscious product. Not a polish item — flagging it as a separate call.

---

# Full inventory

Severity is user impact. Scope is the release vehicle. `file` is where to start.

## A. Canon changes — the design system itself is the defect (40)

Fixing these means editing `dev/design-system/` (DESIGN.md, tokens.css, components.css) and re-syncing to the served CSS. The 0.22 drift check forbids app-side-only edits here — canon first, always. **These are the root causes of "flat, gray, under-designed"; the other sections are largely downstream of them.**

### `global` (22)

- **HIGH** · *color-depth* — **Cards are darker than the ground they sit on in dark theme, so every elevated surface reads as a hole**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/design-system.css:784`
  - *Evidence:* Pixel-sampled desktop-dark/health.png: card interior (700,450) = rgb(24,24,37) = `--bg-mantle` #181825; the ground in the gap between the two card columns (851,450) = rgb(35,34,50); ground left of the grid (370,450) = rgb(31,31,47). The card is 7–11 levels DARKER than its surroundings. Cause is the canon ladder itself: DESIGN.md's surface-ladder table assigns `bg-mantle` #181825 to "Cards, sidebar, topbar" and `bg-base` #1e1e2e to "Page background", i.e. cards are specified one rung BELOW the page. The compensating `border-top-color: var(--border-highlight)` (design-system.css:787, rgba(255,255,255,0.08) ≈ +18 on a single 1px row) is far too weak to reverse the read, and `--shadow-sm` cannot lift a surface that is darker than its backdrop. This is the single largest contributor to the "flat and gray" verdict: the health page is 6 cards, all reading as recessed wells.
  - *Fix:* In DESIGN.md's surface ladder, swap the dark-theme card surface up one rung (card = `bg-base` or `color-mix(bg-base, bg-surface0)`; page ground = `bg-mantle`/`bg-crust`), keeping the light-theme mapping as-is. Alternatively strengthen `.card` with a real top-lit vertical gradient rather than a 1px hairline.

- **HIGH** · *color-depth* — **Card-vs-ground separation is 1.02:1–1.10:1 — below JND — because the body gradient is painted from the same three tokens the cards are made of**
  - *Evidence:* tokens.css:13-20 puts crust #11111b, mantle #181825 and base #1e1e2e within ΔL(oklab) 0.033 of each other (crust→mantle ΔL 0.0327 / 1.068:1; mantle→base ΔL 0.0273 / 1.070:1), while base→surface0 is ΔL 0.0812. components.css:56 then paints the ground as `linear-gradient(170deg, crust → base → mantle)` — i.e. the ground's own endpoints ARE the card colour. Measured on desktop-dark/projects.png: ground directly above the first card (700,130) = #1a1a26 (L=0.2236) vs card interior (700,200) = #181825 (L=0.2155) → ΔL 0.0081, contrast 1.020:1. Mid-page the ground rises to #202030 (L=0.2512) → 1.096:1, and at the very top of health.png the ground is #191924 vs card #181825 → 1.008:1. Light inverts: desktop-light/projects.png ground (700,130) #e3e6ec (L=0.9244) is DARKER than the card (700,200) #e6e9ef (L=0.9335), so the same card is lighter than its ground at the top of the viewport and darker at the middle. Every card boundary on health/tasks/projects/scheduling/knowledge-hub is therefore carried entirely by the 1px #313244 border.
  - *Fix:* Canon revision in tokens.css: give cards a surface that is ≥0.06 ΔL from the page ground (either raise --bg-base / --bg-sub-base toward surface0 for the ground, or introduce a dedicated card token above mantle), and stop the body gradient (components.css:56) short of the card tone — end it at crust→base rather than crust→base→mantle so the ground never equals or exceeds the card surface.

- **HIGH** · *color-depth* — **All colour in the card family is hover-gated, so the resting state of every page is uniformly neutral — 0.50%–1.45% of content pixels carry any chroma**
  - *Evidence:* design-system.css:1019-1029 states it outright: "The tint is hover-only — at rest, these look like standard cards." `.card-metric--accent/-info/-error/-warning` (978-1016) put their radial glow only on `:hover`; `.card:hover` (795-802) is the only place the accent gradient appears; even the hover tint is a 3% mix (`color-mix(in oklab, var(--bg-mantle) 97%, var(--accent))`, line 1034). Measured chroma coverage of the content area (x≥260, y≥48), counting pixels with oklab chroma > 0.035 — a threshold deliberately set just above the most chromatic NEUTRAL token (--bg-surface2 #585b70 = 0.0340): desktop-dark health 0.50%, projects 0.86%, knowledge-hub 1.32%, tasks 1.43%, scheduling 1.45%; desktop-light health 0.24%, tasks 0.97%, scheduling 1.04%. On the health dashboard, 99.5% of the page is less colourful than a border token.
  - *Fix:* Move a fraction of each variant's treatment to the rest state: give `.card-metric--{color}` a permanent faint radial wash and a coloured 1px top edge, and let `.card-tint-{color}` carry ~2–3% of its hue at rest instead of 0. Keep hover as the amplification, not the only appearance.

- **HIGH** · *component-adoption* — **The canon has no form primitives at all — no input, label, checkbox, radio, or toggle rules — yet templates use a bespoke form family 250+ times; § Native selects is documented with zero backing CSS**
  - `dev/design-system/components.css`
  - *Evidence:* components.css contains no `input`, `select`, `textarea`, `label`, `fieldset`, or checkbox/toggle rule of any kind — the only input-family CSS is `.input-area textarea` (components.css:534-570), which is chat-specific. Verified identically on the served copy static/design-system.css. DESIGN.md:662-666 nonetheless documents a "Native selects" component (input-family surface, custom DartClaw chevron, extra right padding, divider before chevron) that no stylesheet implements, and showcase.html has no form section at all. The app therefore carries its own: 56 rule definitions in app.css (`.form-input`, `.form-select`, `.form-label`, `.form-row`, `.form-group`, `.form-error`, `.custom-select*`, `.toggle-switch`, `.toggle-slider`, `.login-input`, `.pairing-input`), used across templates 42x `.form-input`, 55x `.form-label`, 58x `.form-group`, 20x `.form-select`, 31x `.form-error`, 13x `.form-row`, 7x `.custom-select`, 4x `.toggle-switch`. Settings (31KB template), task form, project form, scheduling, guard editor and both pairing pages are therefore entirely outside the design system.
  - *Fix:* Add a Forms section to components.css + DESIGN.md § Components and showcase.html: `.form-field`/`label`/`input`/`select`/`textarea` on the input family (bg-base, inset-sm, radius sm, accent caret, quiet focus), `.form-error`, and a checkbox/toggle. Back the already-documented § Native selects with real CSS, then migrate app.css's `.form-*`/`.custom-select*`/`.toggle-*` onto it.

- **HIGH** · *component-adoption* — **No dialog/modal primitive exists in canon at all — components.css names "modals" as a use case for two card variants but ships no frame, backdrop, or action-row recipe, so the app privately invented one**
  - *Evidence:* dev/design-system/components.css declares 24 numbered component categories in its header (lines 6-30) — none is dialogs/modals — and contains zero occurrences of `dialog`, `showModal`, `::backdrop`, `role="dialog"`, or `aria-modal`; same for showcase.html (the only two 'modal' hits in each file are prose). DESIGN.md:564-565 promises the tier — `| .card-elevated | … | modals, dropdowns, popovers |` and `| .card-glass | translucent + backdrop blur | overlays above live content: modals, command palettes |` — and DESIGN.md:498 even names "modal frames" as a `rounded.lg` consumer, but no such component is defined. Consequence: the app grew its own, app-only: static/app.css:1943-2059 defines `.task-dialog`, `.task-dialog::backdrop` (color-mix scrim + 4px blur), `-header`, `-body`, `-footer`, `-actions`, `.btn-close`, plus a 768px width clamp — ~40 declarations of modal chrome that live outside the design system. It is consumed by templates/task_form.dart:46 and templates/project_form.dart:7, both as `<dialog id="…" class="task-dialog card card-glass">`. Two production modals exist and neither is canon.
  - *Fix:* Promote the proven recipe into dev/design-system/components.css as a new numbered section: `.dialog` (+ `.dialog::backdrop` scrim), `.dialog-header` / `-body` / `-footer` / `-actions`, a `--sm|--md` width ladder, and a `.dialog--confirm` variant (icon + message + `btn-ghost` cancel / `btn-danger-fill` confirm). Add a showcase.html panel demonstrating it, since showcase is the reference for what the system can do. Then reduce app.css `.task-dialog` to a thin alias (or delete it) and point `task_form.dart` / `project_form.dart` at the canonical classes.

- **HIGH** · *dialog-feedback* — **DESIGN.md's feedback decision table sanctions `confirm()` and has no row for input-prompts or blocking errors — the single upstream blocker for every native-dialog defect below**
  - *Evidence:* dev/design-system/DESIGN.md:668-676 §"Banners and toasts" is a 4-row table: `| Persistent problem | Banner (.banner-error/-warning/-info) | … |` / `| Transient success | Toast (.toast-success) | … |` / `| Transient error | Toast (.toast-error) | … |` / line 675 verbatim: `| Destructive confirmation | `confirm()` dialog | Delete session |`. There is no row for "needs user input" (which is why `window.prompt` leaked in unclassified at dc_settings_controller.js:569,573) and no row for "blocking system error" (why `alert()` leaked in at dc_shell_controller.js:488,489,491). The 0.22 PRD correctly treated this row as binding and deferred the work three separate times: dartclaw-private/docs/specs/0.22/prd.md:29 (out of scope), :63, :197, and the decision-log row at :220 — "Keep `window.confirm` … DESIGN.md currently sanctions it … Replace with a glass modal — deferred; requires a DESIGN.md decision-table change first". The 0.22 compliance audit (audit-design-system-compliance.md:160) likewise marked all confirm sites "Compliant as specced". So the app is not violating canon — canon is the defect.
  - *Fix:* Rewrite the DESIGN.md:670-675 table to five rows and delete the `confirm()` sanction: Persistent problem → banner; Transient success/error → toast; **Destructive confirmation → `.dialog.dialog--confirm`** (glass modal, danger-styled primary, object named in the body); **Row-scoped destructive confirmation → `.delete-confirm-bar`** (the inline pattern the app already ships); **Needs structured input → `.dialog` with real form controls; never `window.prompt`**. Add an explicit "Native `alert()`/`confirm()`/`prompt()` are banned" line, mirroring the existing §Native selects prohibition style. This one edit unblocks findings 2-8.

- **HIGH** · *dialog-feedback* — **Six destructive `confirm()` surfaces render as unstyled OS dialogs, including the three highest-consequence actions in the app (reset session, cancel workflow, reject approval)**
  - *Evidence:* Six confirm-class sites, none themeable, all blocking the JS event loop, none capturable by the visual-validation screenshot suite: JS — dc_shell_controller.js:369 `confirm('Permanently delete this chat and all its messages?')` (irreversible session+message delete), dc_shell_controller.js:477 `confirm('Restart ' + appName + '?…')` (whole-server restart), dc_scheduling_controller.js:403 `window.confirm('Delete scheduled task "' + taskId + '"?')` (irreversible config mutation; also shows the raw slug `visual-review-seed` while the table column shows the title "Visual review seed task" — see shots/desktop-dark/scheduling.png), dc_projects_controller.js:192 `window.confirm("Remove project '" + projectName + "'? Running tasks will be cancelled.")` (cancels live work). HTMX — templates/topbar.html:16 `hx-confirm="Reset this session? Conversation will be archived."`, templates/workflow_detail.html:66 `hx-confirm="Cancel this workflow? Running tasks will be stopped."`, :78 `hx-confirm="Reject this approval? The workflow will be cancelled."`. The last two sit directly on the approval-gate/pipeline surfaces that DESIGN.md §Approval Gates (:707-728) designs in fine detail — the app renders a pulsing accent-edged approval card and then hands the actual irreversible decision to a grey OS box with "OK"/"Cancel". None of the six can show `btn-danger-fill`, which the app's own inline confirm bar does use (dc_scheduling_controller.js:200).
  - *Fix:* After the canon change (finding 1), route all six through one `dc-confirm` API: replace the four JS `confirm()` calls with an awaited `confirmDialog({title, body, confirmLabel, danger:true})` in shared.js rendering `<dialog class="dialog dialog--confirm card card-glass">`. For the three `hx-confirm` templates, add a single `htmx:confirm` listener in dc_shell_controller.js that calls `event.preventDefault()` and re-issues `event.detail.issueRequest()` on accept — zero template edits required, and it converts all future `hx-confirm` uses automatically. Also pass the scheduled task's *title*, not its id, into the message.

- **HIGH** · *responsive* — **The single 900px `container-max` breaks data-table headers mid-word while 140px of empty gutter sits on each side**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/tokens.css:106`
  - *Evidence:* tokens.css:106 defines one width primitive, `--container-max: 900px`, applied globally by `.page-inner`/`.content-inner` (app.css:600, design-system.css:1556). At the 1440px capture width the main column is ~1,180px, so the content is pinned to 900px with ~140px of unused ground each side. desktop-dark/tasks.png (crop x395-1315, y500-580) shows the resulting compression: the task-table header wraps mid-word to "PROVID / ER", "CREATE / D" and "TO / KE / NS", inflating the header row to three lines. DESIGN.md § Layout primitives justifies 900px as "for reading comfort" and offers no wider tier, so dense tabular surfaces (tasks, workflows, audit, health metrics) have no compliant option.
  - *Fix:* Add a second layout primitive to DESIGN.md and tokens.css — e.g. `--container-wide: 1280px` — and a `.content-inner--wide` modifier for tabular/dashboard pages; keep 900px for prose and chat. Also add `white-space: nowrap` to `.data-table th`.

- **HIGH** · *responsive* — **A single 900px `container-max` is applied to reading surfaces and data-dense surfaces alike — 280px of a 1440px viewport sits empty while tables and grids are crushed**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/tokens.css:106`
  - *Evidence:* DESIGN.md:396 states "The content column constrains to `container-max` (900px) for reading comfort"; tokens.css:106 defines the single value. Both `.content-inner` (design-system.css) and the parallel `.page-inner` (app.css:600) cap at it. At 1440px the main column is 1180px, minus `--sp-6` padding on both sides = 1132px usable; 900px is used, so ~232px is dead — measurable as the empty gutters at x<400 and x>1300 on every desktop screenshot (health.png, tasks.png, workflows.png, projects.png, scheduling.png). Simultaneously constrained inside that 900px: the 8-column Tasks table (which shatters its headers, see the separate finding), `.card-grid { grid-template-columns: repeat(2, 1fr) }` (app.css:571) which leaves the Health page's 5th service card (Pub/Sub) alone beside an empty cell, and `.metrics-grid-5` (app.css:1033) packing five KPI tiles into 900px. The canon has one width token for a chat transcript and for an operations dashboard; adopting it faithfully is what produces both the dead margins and the crushed tables.
  - *Fix:* Add a second layout tier to the canon (`--container-wide: 1280px`, documented in DESIGN.md § Layout beside `container-max`) and apply it via a `.content-inner--wide` / `.page-inner--wide` modifier on the data surfaces: tasks, health, memory, scheduling, workflows, audit. Keep 900px for chat, session-info, knowledge results, and settings forms where line length matters.

- **HIGH** · *typography* — **Type scale spends 3 of its 7 steps inside a 2px band (12/13/14px) that absorbs ~90% of all declarations**
  - `dev/design-system/tokens.css:71`
  - *Evidence:* tokens.css:71-77 defines --text-xs 12px, --text-sm 13px, --text-base 14px. In JetBrains Mono the cap-height delta from 12→13px is ~0.73px and 13→14px ~0.73px — sub-pixel, not perceivable side by side. Real distribution: app.css 103 xs + 79 sm + 12 base = 194 of 215 font-size declarations (90.2%); design-system.css 27 + 19 + 8 = 54 of 61 (88.5%). Rendered proof in shots/desktop-dark/health.png: the card title "Worker" (design-system.css:805-808, --text-sm bold), the row label "State" and the row value "idle" (app.css:582-584, --text-sm) are all 13px — three semantic levels at one size, separated only by weight 500 and color. shots/desktop-dark/tasks.png: table header 12px, cell 13px, group heading 14px, topbar title 14px — the entire page lives in the 2px band. The scale's step ratios are 1.083 / 1.077 / 1.14 / 1.25 / 1.2 / 1.33: the bottom two steps are below the ~1.125 threshold at which a size change reads as a hierarchy change, so the system has effectively 5 usable sizes, not 7, and the app only reaches 4 of them.
  - *Fix:* Collapse the bottom cluster in dev/design-system/tokens.css: keep --text-xs 12px (caption/metadata) and --text-base 14px (body), and retire --text-sm as a distinct tier (alias it to --text-base during migration). Re-space the middle so heading-md moves to 17-18px, giving a perceptible 14 → 18 → 22 → 28 → 36 ladder. Update the DESIGN.md § Typography table (lines 383-392) to the reduced tier set so `body-sm` stops being a legitimate choice.

- **HIGH** · *typography* — **DESIGN.md names 8 typographic tiers but the CSS ships only raw size tokens and two utilities — both of them the smallest sizes**
  - `dev/design-system/components.css:1627`
  - *Evidence:* DESIGN.md:383-392 specifies 8 tiers each with fontSize + fontWeight + lineHeight + letterSpacing (frontmatter lines 75-117 repeat them as machine-readable values). components.css implements exactly ONE of them as a reusable class: `.card-metric .metric-value` (components.css:977-984). There is no `.display`, `.page-title`, `.heading-md`, `.label-md`, `.body-md`, `.body-sm`, `.caption` class anywhere. The only typographic utilities shipped are `.text-xs` and `.text-sm` (components.css:1627-1628) — the two smallest steps; there is no `.text-base/-lg/-xl/-2xl/-3xl`. showcase.html:209-217 demonstrates the scale as seven bare `font-size:` inline styles with no weight/leading, so there is nothing to copy either. Direct downstream cost, measurable in app.css: of 21 rules at ≥16px, 15 declare no line-height and inherit `html { line-height: 1.6 }` (see separate finding); `--tracking-tight` appears exactly once in 3827 lines despite canon (DESIGN.md:381) requiring it on all display/metric text. tokens.css defines --leading/--leading-tight/--tracking-tight/--tracking-caps as four unbound primitives, so a correct tier requires hand-assembling 4 declarations every time — and it is not being done.
  - *Fix:* Add a composite type layer to dev/design-system/components.css: one class per named tier (`.t-display`, `.t-page-title`, `.t-heading`, `.t-body`, `.t-label`, `.t-caption`, `.t-metric`) that binds font-size + font-weight + line-height + letter-spacing together exactly as the DESIGN.md frontmatter declares, and demonstrate each in showcase.html § Typography. Then the app applies a class rather than re-deriving four properties. Keep the raw --text-* tokens for one-offs only.

- med · *color-depth* — **The "atmospheric" ground delivers ~2.7% luminance variation across a full viewport — the glows are anchored off-canvas**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/design-system.css:52`
  - *Evidence:* design-system.css:52-60 declares three radial glows plus a 170deg 3-stop gradient, and DESIGN.md § Body background asserts "The ground is atmospheric, not flat" and "The glows are what keep large empty regions from reading flat". Measured on desktop-dark/settings.png, x=350 column: y=100 → rgb(25,27,37), y=500 → rgb(32,32,47), y=890 → rgb(30,31,46); x=1430 column: y=55 → rgb(31,31,44), y=890 → rgb(28,28,40). Total swing across 1440×900 is 7/255 ≈ 2.7% — at the perceptual floor. Two causes, both in canon: (a) all three glow centres are placed outside the box (`at 12% -8%`, `at 90% -10%`, `at 50% 112%`, design-system.css:53-55) so only their faint tails are on screen; (b) `--ambient-a/-b/-c` are capped at 7%/6%/5% mix (tokens.css:60-62). Light theme is worse: rgb(222,226,231) → rgb(230,233,239), a 3-level swing.
  - *Fix:* Pull at least one glow centre on-canvas (e.g. `at 20% 10%`), widen the ellipses, and/or raise `--ambient-*` to ~10-12% mix; then re-measure — the target should be a ≥12/255 swing corner-to-corner in dark.

- med · *color-depth* — **The ambient ground is measurably invisible: total corner-to-corner variation is ΔE(oklab) 0.0185 in both themes, and app.css references the ambient tokens zero times**
  - *Evidence:* tokens.css:58-60 caps the three glows at 7%/6%/5% mix; components.css:53-56 places them off-canvas at 12% -8%, 90% -10% and 50% 112%. Measured across the full content ground of desktop-dark/health.png: top-left #1b1c24 (L=0.2290, chroma 0.0155) vs top-right #1e1d2d (L=0.2397, chroma 0.0304) → ΔE_oklab 0.0185; desktop-light/health.png top-left #dbe0e4 (chroma 0.0077) vs top-right #e3e4ed (chroma 0.0122) → ΔE_oklab 0.0185. The full vertical sweep of the base gradient is only ΔL 0.032 (dark, #191924→#202030) and 0.027 (light). Film grain is present but at --noise-opacity 0.04 contributes ±1–2/255 per channel. app.css uses --ambient-a/-b/-c 0 times and --noise 0 times, so nothing in the app layer reinforces it. Net: the "atmospheric ground" the spec relies on (DESIGN.md § Body background, § Density) delivers a hue shift roughly half of what a large uniform field needs to register.
  - *Fix:* Canon revision: raise the ambient mixes to ~12–16% and pull the glow centres inside the viewport (e.g. 18% 6%, 85% 4%) so the ground carries a visible hue gradient; verify by re-measuring corner-to-corner ΔE_oklab ≥ 0.04.

- med · *color-depth* — **Elevation is one pixel row: shadow-sm renders a single-row band, so the whole depth story is 1px border + 1px highlight + 1px shadow**
  - *Evidence:* tokens.css:121 defines `--shadow-sm: 0 1px 3px rgba(9,9,26,0.50), 0 1px 2px rgba(9,9,26,0.30)`. Vertical scan of desktop-dark/health.png at x=600 across a card's bottom edge: y=627 border #313244, y=628 #141424 (the shadow), y=629 already back to ground #191929 — one row, ΔL 0.042 against ground. The top highlight is likewise one row (y=492 #2a2a36, ΔL 0.075 above the card) and reads as a lighter border rather than as a lit edge. In light the shadow row is ΔL 0.022. With the surface step at 1.02–1.10:1 (finding 1), a 1px shadow is the entire elevation budget.
  - *Fix:* Canon revision: widen shadow-sm to a two-layer ambient+key recipe with real blur (e.g. `0 1px 2px rgba(9,9,26,.5), 0 4px 10px -2px rgba(9,9,26,.4)`) so a resting card casts a readable band, and reserve the current value for `.chip`/`.kbd`-scale atoms.

- med · *color-depth* — **The canon defines a --chart-1..6 ramp but ships no chart, sparkline, or trend component — so every numeric surface can only be a label/value row, a .card-metric, or a .meter**
  - `dev/design-system/components.css`
  - *Evidence:* DESIGN.md:359 mandates the ordered categorical ramp ("assign by series index, never by hand-picking — the order keeps adjacent series distinguishable and charts consistent across views") and tokens.css:52-57 defines `--chart-1`…`--chart-6`. But grep of components.css, DESIGN.md and showcase.html for `sparkline`, `bar-chart` or a `.chart` component returns zero hits — showcase.html only paints the six values as color swatches (line 122-127). Consequently `var(--chart-N)` is referenced 0 times in app.css, and so are `--mauve`, `--sky`, `--pink`, `--lavender` (only `--teal` 3x). Surfaces that obviously want it and don't have it: memory_dashboard.html "Recent Runs" (5-row archived/deduped/remaining/size table, desktop-dark/memory.png), health_dashboard.html Metrics row, and the workflow token/duration KPIs — all rendered as static numbers.
  - *Fix:* Add a minimal data-viz primitive to components.css + showcase.html — a CSS-only `.sparkline`/`.bar-track` + `.bar-seg` set driven by `--chart-1..6` by series index — then use it for memory pruning history and health metrics. Without it the ramp token is unreachable and dashboards stay label/value only.

- med · *color-depth* — **The --fg-overlay token itself misses AA in both themes even in its sanctioned helper-text role, so the canon has no compliant low-emphasis tier**
  - `dev/design-system/tokens.css:28,179`
  - *Evidence:* tokens.css:28 dark `--fg-overlay: #7f849c` → 4.44:1 on --bg-base, 3.92:1 on --bg-sub-base, 3.40:1 on --bg-surface0. tokens.css:179 light `--fg-overlay: #7b8094` → 3.47:1 on --bg-base, 3.22:1 on --bg-mantle, 2.54:1 on --bg-surface0. DESIGN.md:804 claims 'maintain WCAG AA contrast — representative pairings were tuned for both themes; fg-overlay is helper/disabled text only', but AA 4.5 applies to helper text too (only genuinely disabled controls are exempt), and the token misses it on every surface in light and on all but crust/pit in dark. Consequence: there is no token between --fg-sub0 and disabled, so the app reaches for --fg-overlay whenever it wants 'quieter than sub0' — which is why finding #3 exists at all.
  - *Fix:* Darken light `--fg-overlay` to ~#6c7186 (≥4.5:1 on --bg-mantle) and lighten dark `--fg-overlay` to ~#8b90a8 (≥4.5:1 on --bg-base), then re-sync tokens.css to the served copy. Alternatively split the tier: keep `--fg-overlay` as a true disabled-only token and add a compliant `--fg-muted` for helper text, and say so in DESIGN.md § Accessibility.

- med · *component-adoption* — **btn-sm is the most-used button modifier in the app (58 uses) but the canon defines no button size tier at all**
  - `dev/design-system/components.css`
  - *Evidence:* DESIGN.md:612-620 enumerates the complete button vocabulary — `.btn`, `.btn-primary`, `.btn-ghost`, `.btn-danger`, `.btn-icon`, `.btn-full` — with no size dimension. grep of dev/design-system/components.css, DESIGN.md and showcase.html for `btn-sm` returns 0 hits in all three. Yet across templates/ and controllers/ `btn-sm` appears 58 times (more than `btn-ghost` at 46 and `btn-primary` at 43), backed only by app.css:569 `.btn-sm { font-size: var(--text-xs); padding: var(--sp-1) var(--sp-3); }`, plus `.btn-icon-sm` (app.css:788, 4 uses) and off-vocabulary `.btn-secondary` (2), `.btn-stop`, `.btn-close`, `.btn-accept`, `.btn-reject`, `.btn-pushback`, `.btn-danger-fill`, `.btn-active`, `.btn-reset`, `.btn-login`. Meanwhile canon's own `.btn-full` has 0 uses. The single most-repeated control in the product is therefore ungoverned, and its 12px label is a direct contributor to the 85%-at-12-13px type distribution.
  - *Fix:* Add a sanctioned size tier to components.css/DESIGN.md § Buttons (e.g. `.btn-sm` at `--text-sm` 13px with a defined min-height, and `.btn-icon-sm`) and show it in showcase.html; then fold `.btn-secondary`/`.btn-accept`/`.btn-reject`/`.btn-stop`/`.btn-close`/`.btn-danger-fill` onto `.btn`+`.btn-ghost`/`-primary`/`-danger` and delete them from app.css.

- med · *dialog-feedback* — **The toast — the app's only feedback surface, used at ~86 call sites — distinguishes success from error solely by a 3px left border color, with no icon and no title**
  - *Evidence:* dev/design-system/components.css:1454-1477 defines `.toast` as a single flex row: `border-left: 3px solid var(--fg-overlay)` overridden per variant (`.toast-success/-error/-warning/-info` at :1474-1477 change *only* `border-left-color`), `font-size: var(--text-sm)` (13px), one text run and a dismiss button. No icon slot, no weight/tier contrast between a title and a detail. shared.js:`showToast` renders exactly that — `'<span>' + escapeHtml(message) + '</span>' + '<button class="toast-dismiss" …>'` — no icon element. showcase.html:1130-1145 builds the same bare structure. So at a glance a 13px "Applied" and a 13px "Validation failed" differ by three pixels of colored edge, on the surface that carries essentially all app feedback (~86 `showToast` calls: dc_settings 38, dc_scheduling 22, dc_shell 8, dc_tasks 9, dc_projects 5). Meanwhile DESIGN.md:860-875 defines exactly the icons this needs — `circle-check` (success), `triangle-alert` (warning), `circle-x`, `info` — and §Approval Gates / §Panels use leading-icon + edge together everywhere else in the system. The toast is the one severity-bearing component in canon that carries severity on the edge alone.
  - *Fix:* Amend components.css `.toast` to a two-column grid (leading `.icon` + body) and have each variant set both the border color and a `--icon-*` mask, per the DESIGN.md:860-875 mapping; optionally add an optional bold `.toast-title`. Update showcase.html and shared.js `showToast` to emit `<span class="icon icon-circle-check">` etc. from the type argument (the type is already sanitized at shared.js via `sanitizeClassToken`).

- med · *state-coverage* — **Canon .empty-state defines only a container and an icon — no title, action, or absent-value tier, which is why the app invented eight replacements**
  - `dev/design-system/components.css:1506`
  - *Evidence:* design-system.css:1506-1527 is the entire empty-state canon: `.empty-state` (flex column, `--fg-sub0`), `.empty-state .icon` (accent + glow), `.empty-state p` (max-width, `--text-sm`). There is no title style, no action-slot spacing, and no guidance on which icon tier (mascot vs `.claw-mark` vs `❯_` vs the sanctioned emoji at DESIGN.md:895) applies when. The canon's own reference implementation has to patch around this: showcase.html:906 writes `<strong style="color:var(--fg)">No sessions yet</strong>` — an inline style override inside the design system's own showcase. The app's three icon treatments (claw-mark, ❯_, 💬) and eight bespoke classes are the downstream consequence. The canon also has no zero-value treatment for `.meter` (design-system.css:1331) — at 0% it is an inert `--bg-crust` line, visible in desktop-dark/memory.png where the LEARNINGS 0/50 meter reads as a solid rule.
  - *Fix:* Extend the canon: add `.empty-state-title` (--fg, weight 600, `display` tier per DESIGN.md:391), define the action-button slot spacing, and add a decision rule for icon tier (mascot for page-level, claw-mark for section-level, none for inline/table cells). Add a `.meter--empty` zero state. Then delete the showcase's inline style override.

- med · *typography* — **No measure rule anywhere in canon; container-max 900px yields ~103 characters per line, ~40% over a comfortable measure**
  - `dev/design-system/DESIGN.md:396`
  - *Evidence:* DESIGN.md:396 states "The content column constrains to `container-max` (900px) for reading comfort" and DESIGN.md:413 repeats it, but neither DESIGN.md nor tokens.css nor components.css contains any character-based measure rule — grep for 'measure', 'line length', 'ch;' across dev/design-system/ returns nothing. JetBrains Mono's advance is 0.6em, so at the 14px body tier 900px minus `.msg` padding (sp-4 each side, design-system.css:435-441) = 868px = 103.3 characters per line; `.content-inner` (design-system.css:1554-1556, also 900px) gives 107ch for un-carded prose. The comfortable range is 45-75ch, and mono UIs conventionally cap at 80ch. Verifiable in shots/desktop-dark/knowledge-hub.png: the README snippet runs the full 868px, ~103 characters, before wrapping. The 900px value is currently justified in canon prose *as* a reading-comfort constraint while producing the opposite.
  - *Fix:* Add a measure token to dev/design-system/tokens.css (`--measure: 72ch`) and a § Typography rule stating that running prose (.msg-content, .card-body, .page-subtitle, knowledge snippets) constrains to --measure while tabular/dense layouts keep --container-max. Apply `max-width: var(--measure)` to .msg-content and .knowledge-result-snippet.

- low · *layout-spacing* — **Canon defines no z-index/stacking scale, so the app invented five ad-hoc layers — and the toast layer (z-index 100) is below both the restart overlay (9999) and the browser top layer used by `<dialog>`**
  - *Evidence:* dev/design-system/DESIGN.md and dev/design-system/tokens.css contain zero occurrences of `z-index`; §Elevation & Depth (DESIGN.md:474-489) stops at shadow tokens and the glass tier and says nothing about stacking order, despite naming modals, popovers, command palettes and toasts as tiers. Canon components.css hardcodes 20 / 15 (sidebar + scrim, :115,:134) and 100 (`.toast-container`, :1450). The app layer then adds its own unrelated values: static/app.css:12 (20), :638 (1), :855 (30), :1176 (10), :1905 (10), :2008 (`.restart-overlay` 9999), :3228 (30). Concrete consequence: any toast raised while a native `<dialog>` is open is invisible, because `showModal()` promotes the dialog to the browser top layer, which beats any z-index — this is latent today only because both existing dialogs (`new-task-dialog`, `add-project-dialog`) deliberately route their errors to inline `.form-error` nodes instead (dc_tasks_controller.js:790, dc_projects_controller.js:122,136,158), and it becomes a live bug the moment confirm/prompt replacements start toasting from inside a dialog.
  - *Fix:* Add a `--z-*` token ladder to tokens.css (base / sticky / sidebar / dropdown / overlay / toast) plus a stacking-order paragraph in DESIGN.md §Elevation & Depth, and make the toast tier the highest non-top-layer value. For dialogs specifically, document the rule that a toast container must be re-parented into the open `<dialog>` (or the dialog must use inline `.form-error`) so feedback raised from a modal remains visible.

- low · *typography* — **An all-monospace system at 12-14px has no hierarchy device left except weight and CASE — and the declared sans token is dead**
  - `dev/design-system/tokens.css:69`
  - *Evidence:* DESIGN.md:376-381 commits the entire system to one family and three weights. Because 90% of type also sits in the 2px 12-14px band (see the scale finding), the only remaining differentiators are weight (app.css uses 41 bold + 42 medium + 1 normal — a near 50/50 split, so 'bold' has no scarcity value) and text-transform. The result is that uppercase+tracking-caps becomes the de facto heading device: `letter-spacing: var(--tracking-caps)` appears 27 times in app.css and 9 in design-system.css, versus `--tracking-tight` once. In shots/desktop-dark/health.png the only two headings on screen are "SERVICES" and "METRICS", both 12px uppercase `.section-label` — the smallest text on the page is doing the job of the largest. Meanwhile `--font-sans: system-ui, -apple-system, 'Segoe UI', sans-serif` is declared in both dev/design-system/tokens.css:69 and the served packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/tokens.css:71 and referenced exactly zero times in any CSS, HTML, or JS in the repo — the system already carries the escape hatch it refuses to use.
  - *Fix:* Decide explicitly in DESIGN.md § Typography: either (a) delete --font-sans and accept that hierarchy must come entirely from a widened size scale — which then requires the scale fix — or (b) make --font-sans load-bearing for one narrow role (page/section headings, or running prose in messages and knowledge snippets) so the mono face marks data and the sans face marks chrome. Leaving it declared and unused is the one option that buys nothing.

### `chat-session` (3)

- med · *accessibility* — **The composer's send button never reaches full strength and fails non-text contrast in light theme — it reads as a broken control**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/design-system.css:639`
  - *Evidence:* Sampled from the screenshots (16x16 px at the button centre): light theme fill rgb(161,187,161) vs glyph rgb(231,234,240) = 1.72:1; dark theme fill rgb(86,109,93) vs glyph rgb(25,25,38) = 3.15:1. WCAG 1.4.11 requires ≥3:1 for the graphic that identifies a control. Two stacked opacity rules cause it: `.btn:disabled, .btn[disabled] { opacity: 0.4 }` (design-system.css:374) applies on an empty composer (dc_chat_controller.js:138 disables send when there is no text), and even the enabled resting state is deliberately dimmed — `.composer-send { opacity: 0.6 }` (design-system.css:639-644), mandated by DESIGN.md:634 ("rests at 0.6 opacity and wakes to full"). Composing the 0.6 rule against the light palette (`--accent: #24661c` on `--bg-base`, glyph `--bg-crust: #dce0e8`) yields ~2.4:1 — still under 3:1 — so the primary action of the flagship surface is sub-AA whenever the composer is not focused. DESIGN.md:798 simultaneously requires "maintain WCAG AA contrast", so the canon contradicts itself.
  - *Fix:* Stop expressing the resting state with element-level `opacity` (which collapses fill-vs-glyph contrast); dim the *fill* instead (`background: color-mix(accent 70%, bg-surface0)`) while keeping the glyph at full strength, and re-tune the light-theme `--accent`/glyph pairing to ≥3:1 in both rest and disabled states.

- med · *layout-spacing* — **The message thread never bottom-anchors: on a short conversation ~558px (62%) of the flagship viewport is empty ground**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/design-system.css:417`
  - *Evidence:* `.messages { flex: 1; overflow-y: auto; padding: var(--sp-4); scroll-behavior: smooth }` (design-system.css:417-422) — no `justify-content: flex-end`, no `margin-top: auto` on the last child. In shots/desktop-dark/chat-session.png (1440x900) the last message ends at y≈222 and the `.input-area` top border sits at y≈780, leaving ~558px of unbroken flat background between the conversation and the composer. The same gap dominates all four captures. The served rule is byte-identical to the canon (dev/design-system/components.css:415-420 — diff of the whole CHAT section is empty), so faithful adoption produces this; the canon has no answer here. This is the single largest contributor to the "flat and gray" verdict on the most-used page.
  - *Fix:* Bottom-anchor the thread in the canon: add `display:flex; flex-direction:column; justify-content:flex-end` (or `.msg:first-child { margin-top: auto }`) to `.messages` so short conversations sit against the composer, and give the surface something above the fold — session header card, or the empty-state/mascot moment — instead of raw ground.

- med · *typography* — **Markdown h1/h2/h3 inside agent messages all collapse to one size and weight, erasing structure from every generated document**
  - `dev/design-system/components.css:472`
  - *Evidence:* design-system.css:472-477 (canon components.css:472): `.msg-content h1, .msg-content h2, .msg-content h3 { font-size: var(--text-lg); font-weight: var(--weight-bold); ... }`. Three heading levels are rendered identically at 16px/600, and h4-h6 get no rule at all so they fall through to UA defaults (h4 1em bold, h5 0.83em, h6 0.67em) — i.e. h4 lands at 14px, SMALLER than the h3 above it is meant to be but the same as body, and h6 renders at ~9px. The chat surface is where the product's primary output (agent-written plans, reviews, summaries) is read; shots/desktop-dark/chat-session.png shows `.msg-content` body at 14px with no heading present, but any multi-section agent response renders as a flat wall. The knowledge surface has the mirror problem: shots/desktop-dark/knowledge-hub.png shows a raw "# Wiki" leaking into the snippet unrendered.
  - *Fix:* In dev/design-system/components.css, split the rule: h1 → --text-xl/600/leading-tight, h2 → --text-lg/600/leading-tight, h3 → --text-base/600, h4-h6 → --text-base/500 with --fg-sub1, and add explicit margins so the levels differ by space as well as size. Sync to packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/design-system.css.

### `shell/topbar` (2)

- med · *typography* — **The topbar page title renders at 14px — smaller than the sidebar wordmark and smaller than the in-page h1 it duplicates**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/design-system.css:317`
  - *Evidence:* `.topbar .session-title-static { font-size: var(--text-base) }` = 14px (design-system.css:317-329) and `.topbar .session-title { font-size: var(--text-base) }` = 14px (design-system.css:297). DESIGN.md's typography table reserves `page-title` = 20px/600 for "App name, page title" and tokens.css:77 defines `--text-xl: 1.25rem /* 20px — page title */`. Meanwhile `.sidebar-header .logo` is `--text-lg` 16px (design-system.css:155-163) and the in-page `.page-title` is `--text-xl` 20px (app.css:608). So on Settings and Knowledge Hub the global page header (14px) is the smallest of three competing titles on the same screen. app.css:278-287 re-declares `.session-title-static` at `--weight-medium` / `--fg-sub1`, but it is dead code — the canon's `.topbar .session-title-static` has higher specificity (0,2,0 vs 0,1,0); verified by sampling: the "Settings" title peaks at grayscale 215, identical to `--fg` body text, not `--fg-sub1`.
  - *Fix:* Raise `.topbar .session-title-static` / `.topbar .session-title` to `--text-xl` (20px) / `--weight-bold` to match DESIGN.md's `page-title` tier, and delete the dead duplicate block at app.css:278-287.

- low · *layout-spacing* — **The topbar carries one control on 4 of 5 surfaces — a 1,180×48px bar that is ~95% empty on every system page**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/templates/topbar.html:32`
  - *Evidence:* topbar.html's `pageTopbar` fragment (lines 32-42) contains only: menu-toggle (hidden ≥769px per design-system.css:294), the title, and `.theme-toggle`. On tasks.png, health.png, settings.png and knowledge-hub.png the entire bar between x≈390 and x≈1395 is empty ground. Only the session topbar (lines 1-21) adds two more controls. Meanwhile every page-level primary action lives inside the scrolling column instead: "New Task" at (765,92) on tasks.png, the search field + "Search" button at y≈261 on knowledge-hub.png, the settings tab strip at y≈195 — all of which scroll away. The canon offers nothing here either: `.topbar-actions` is an empty flex container (design-system.css:331-335) and showcase.html:1062 also demonstrates a topbar containing only the theme toggle.
  - *Fix:* Give the canon a real topbar contract: a `.topbar-actions` slot spec for page-level primary actions plus optional breadcrumb/context (the `backHref` slot in topbar.html:35 already exists but is unused on all top-level pages), then hoist per-page primary actions (New Task, Search, Refresh) out of the scroll column into it.

### `chat/composer` (1)

- med · *accessibility* — **The canon-mandated 'quiet' composer focus removes the ring and leaves a 2.0–2.7:1 border as the only persistent focus cue — below the 3:1 non-text minimum**
  - `dev/design-system/DESIGN.md:630`
  - *Evidence:* DESIGN.md:630 mandates 'Focus is terminal-native and quiet: no rings, no glow', and design-system.css:610 implements it: `.composer textarea:focus { border: none; outline: none; box-shadow: none }`. The three sanctioned cues are the accent caret, the send button waking from 0.6 to 1.0 opacity, and `.composer:focus-within` taking 'a whisper of accent (30% mix into surface0)' on the container border. That border computes to #54675f against --bg-base = 2.72:1 in dark and 2.04:1 in light — under the 3:1 required of a focus indicator (WCAG 1.4.11/2.4.11). The caret is not a persistent indicator (it blinks and vanishes when the window loses and regains focus in some engines), and a 0.6→1.0 opacity shift on a separate button is not a focus indicator for the textarea. Unlike the other findings here, adopting the canon faithfully is what produces the failure.
  - *Fix:* Keep the terminal aesthetic but make the container border carry real weight: raise the focus-within mix to ~55% accent into --bg-surface0 (≥3:1 against --bg-base in both themes) or add a 1px inset accent ring on `.composer:focus-within`. Amend DESIGN.md § Composer to state the 3:1 floor explicitly so 'quiet' is bounded rather than open-ended.

### `global (light theme)` (1)

- **HIGH** · *color-depth* — **Light theme loses depth harder than dark, and sidebar, topbar and cards are all the same token so the shell has no chrome/content separation**
  - *Evidence:* Measured on desktop-light/health.png: card #e6e9ef (L=0.9335) vs mid-page ground #edeff3 (L=0.9517) → ΔL 0.018, contrast 1.056:1 (dark's best case is 1.096:1). The card's drop shadow is one pixel row: #e2e4e9 against ground #eaebf1 → ΔL 0.022 (light `--shadow-sm: 0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,.06)`, tokens.css:204) versus ΔL 0.042 in dark. Sidebar (130,400), topbar (700,23) and card interior (700,560) all measure exactly #e6e9ef — all three are `--bg-mantle` — and the sidebar rail is divided from the content ground by a single #ccd0da pixel column at x=259, a ΔL of 0.010 between the two fields it separates. In dark the same boundary is ΔL 0.027. The rail effectively disappears in light mode.
  - *Fix:* Give the light theme its own surface separation rather than a mirrored ladder: pull `--bg-mantle` down (toward #dee1e9) or push `--bg-base` up to white, strengthen light `--shadow-sm` to ~`0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,.10)`, and give `.sidebar`/`.topbar` a distinct token from `.card` so shell chrome is not the same surface as content.

### `global (observed on projects/workflows)` (1)

- med · *typography* — **The canon itself has no card-title tier: `.card-header` is 13px — smaller than body text — so faithful adoption still produces flat cards**
  - `dev/design-system/components.css:803`
  - *Evidence:* dev/design-system/components.css:803-806 `.card-header { font-size: var(--text-sm); font-weight: var(--weight-bold); }` = 13px bold, one step BELOW the 14px body set on `body` (components.css:51-58). DESIGN.md:383-392 documents `heading-md 16px/600 — Section headings` and `display 24px` and `metric-value 32px`, but nothing between 14px `label-md` and 16px `heading-md` is wired to a card, and `.card-header` — the only card-title recipe in the system — is not on that ladder at all. Across all 2300 lines of components.css `--text-lg` appears 5 times and `--text-3xl` exactly once (`.card-metric`). So a page built strictly to canon (as workflow_list and projects mostly are) tops out at 14px on everything except a KPI tile, which matches the owner's "flat and gray / typography flawed" verdict.
  - *Fix:* Amend DESIGN.md § Cards and components.css: raise `.card-header` to `--text-lg` (16px/600) with `--tracking-tight`, add an explicit `card-title` row to the type table between `label-md` and `heading-md`, and document a compact `.card-header--sm` (13px) for dense list rows so the dense case is opt-in rather than the default.

### `health-dashboard` (1)

- med · *layout-spacing* — **Fixed-column grids orphan the last card on both health grids; canon's dashboard recipe references grid primitives that don't exist**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/app.css:571-574,586-588`
  - *Evidence:* app.css:571-574 `.card-grid { grid-template-columns: repeat(2, 1fr) }` receives 5 service cards (health_dashboard.dart:49-131 builds 4 + Pub/Sub) → Pub/Sub sits alone with a 440px empty cell to its right, visible at (860-1300, 645-810) in desktop-dark/health.png. app.css:586-588 `.metrics-grid { repeat(3, 1fr) }` receives 4 metric cards (health_dashboard.dart:144-149) → the same orphan one row down. Default `align-items: stretch` also inflates the 2-row Worker card to match the 3-row Database card, leaving a visible void at its base (y≈435-475). DESIGN.md § Composition Patterns diagrams the dashboard using `grid-4` and `grid-2` primitives — neither exists in design-system.css or dev/design-system/components.css, and DESIGN.md § Layout primitives lists only sidebar-w / topbar-h / container-max. Every page therefore invents its own fixed-count grid.
  - *Fix:* Ship real grid primitives in components.css (`.grid-2`/`.grid-3`/`.grid-4` on `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(Npx, 1fr))`, matching the `.heartbeat-grid` pattern already at app.css:622) and list them under DESIGN.md § Layout primitives; then point `.card-grid` and `.metrics-grid` at them so 5-card and 4-card sets reflow instead of orphaning. Add `align-content: start` on card cells so short cards don't stretch.

### `kg-timeline` (1)

- med · *component-adoption* — **The category-first "Timeline" has no time axis: chronology is hidden in 12px pills at the bottom of each card**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/templates/kg_timeline.html:44`
  - *Evidence:* kg_timeline.html:44-66 renders each category as a flat `.kg-category-group` stack of `.kg-fact-card`s. The facts ARE chronological (temporal_knowledge_graph_service.dart:134 `ORDER BY entity ASC, valid_from ASC`), but nothing on screen says so: there is no date gutter, no rail, no run-of-time grouping, no oldest→newest cue, and the only temporal data is `.kg-window-pill` "valid_from …" / "valid_to …" at `--text-xs` 12px in `--fg-sub0`, placed *below* the statement (app.css:3376-3392). Category headings — the page's primary structural device — use `<h2 class="section-label">` (kg_timeline.html:46), 12px uppercase `--fg-sub0`, so the grouping reads weaker than the cards inside it. The canon offers no help: DESIGN.md and components.css contain no timeline/chronology component at all, which is why app.css already carries three unrelated hand-rolled ones (`.task-timeline`/`.tl-event` app.css:2130+, `.knowledge-timeline-item`/`.timeline-rail` app.css:3405-3427, and this page's `.kg-*` app.css:3304+).
  - *Fix:* Add one canonical chronology component to dev/design-system (rail + date gutter + node markers + a `.timeline-group` date heading) and adopt it here: promote `valid_from` to a left date gutter, keep `valid_to`/state as the card's trailing metadata, and raise category headings to `.section-title` (16px, `--fg`). Retire the three ad-hoc implementations onto it.

### `knowledge-hub` (1)

- med · *component-adoption* — **The design canon has no form-control, search-toolbar or pagination family, so every list surface invents one**
  - `dev/design-system/components.css`
  - *Evidence:* components.css contains no `input`, `.form-input`, `.form-select`, search, toolbar or pagination rule (grep over the file returns only `.composer textarea`, `.input-area textarea` and `.composer-toolbar`), and DESIGN.md's Components section (lines 507-775) covers wells, cards, status, chips, banners, notifications, palette and orchestration but never a text field, a filter toolbar, or a pager. The reference itself works around the gap: showcase.html:722 inline-styles the palette's search input (`style="flex:1;background:transparent;border:none;outline:none;color:var(--fg);font:inherit"`). The consequences on this surface are the unstyled `input[type=search]`, the bespoke `.knowledge-search-strip` / `.filter-chip-row` (app.css:3064-3102) and the bespoke `.pager` / `.pager-link` / `.pager-label` (app.css:3183-3202) — none of which can be "adopted" because there is nothing to adopt. app.css's `.form-input` is an app-layer invention with no canon backing, which is why a page authored outside the sweep never picked it up.
  - *Fix:* Promote a form-control family into components.css + DESIGN.md — `.form-input` / `.form-select` / `.form-field` (bg-base, `--border`, accent focus-visible ring, `--fg-overlay` placeholder), a search-field variant with a leading `.icon-search`, and a `.pager` pattern — then have app.css consume rather than define them.

### `memory-dashboard` (1)

- **HIGH** · *color-depth* — **Card surface and page ground are the same colour — the canon's ground gradient terminates on the card token**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/design-system.css:51`
  - *Evidence:* Sampled pixels from shots/desktop-*/memory.png at the KPI row (y=150): light — card #e6e9ef vs gap between cards #e5e6ed = 1.005:1; dark — card #181825 vs gap #1b1b28 = 1.02:1, vs page right edge #1d1d2c = 1.06:1. Cause: `body { background: … linear-gradient(170deg, var(--bg-crust) 0%, var(--bg-base) 50%, var(--bg-mantle) 100%) }` (dev/design-system/components.css / served design-system.css:51-57) sweeps *through and ends on* `--bg-mantle`, which is exactly `.card { background: var(--bg-mantle) }` (design-system.css:785). In light theme the ladder is non-monotonic (crust #dce0e8 → base #eff1f5 → mantle #e6e9ef), so the crossing band is wide, and the only remaining separation is a 1px border plus `--shadow-sm: 0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,.06)` (tokens.css:206) — a 2px blur at 6% alpha. Every card on this page is an outline, not a surface. This is the mechanical root of the "flat and gray" verdict, and it is inherited from the canon, not introduced by the app.
  - *Fix:* In dev/design-system: stop the ground gradient on a token that is never a card fill (e.g. crust → base → color-mix(base, crust)) so no page band equals `--bg-mantle`; and raise light-theme `--shadow-sm` to a real elevation (~0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,.10), 0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,.06)). Re-sync design-system.css/tokens.css to packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/.

### `projects, chat composer` (1)

- med · *interaction-glitch* — **Ghost buttons are indistinguishable from static text at rest — no border, no background, no affordance**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/design-system.css:392`
  - *Evidence:* The canon defines `.btn-ghost { background: transparent; border-color: transparent; color: var(--fg-sub0); box-shadow: none; }` with visual state appearing only on `:hover` (static/design-system.css:392-393). Applied to real controls this reads as prose: in shots/desktop-dark/projects.png the 'Fetch' action (templates/projects.html:28-29, `class="btn btn-ghost btn-sm"`) is a bare word inside a card that also contains bare-word metadata, with nothing marking it clickable. Same failure in the chat composer: the 'Open commands' control (templates/chat.html:76-77, `class="btn btn-ghost btn-icon"` with the literal text `/`) renders as an unadorned slash immediately left of the static `<kbd>/</kbd> commands` hint, so shots/desktop-dark/chat-session.png shows what looks like a duplicated '/'. The suggestion chips beside it are worse — `.composer-hint-action` is `background: transparent; border: none` in the same `--fg-sub0`-family colour as the non-interactive `.composer-hints` text (app.css:67-91), so 'Summarize' and "What's changed?" are typographically identical to the shortcut legend. Hover-only affordance is also unreachable on touch, where the mobile breakpoint keeps these controls visible (app.css:102-105).
  - *Fix:* Give `.btn-ghost` a rest-state signal in the canon — a `--bg-surface0` fill or a 1px `--bg-surface1` border — rather than transparent-until-hover; and give `.composer-hint-action` a distinct chip treatment so interactive suggestions never share a style with the static shortcut legend.

### `settings/all-tabs` (1)

- med · *accessibility* — **--fg-overlay helper text fails WCAG AA in the light theme, and it is the settings page's primary explanatory text**
  - `dev/design-system/tokens.css:179`
  - *Evidence:* `app.css:848` `.form-hint { font-size: var(--text-xs); color: var(--fg-overlay) }`, also `app.css:489` `.settings-header p`, `app.css:501` `.card-detail-hint`, `app.css:1212` `.section-note`. In the light theme `--fg-overlay: #7b8094` (tokens.css:179) on the card surface `--bg-mantle: #e6e9ef` (tokens.css:169) gives ≈3.25:1 — below the 4.5:1 AA threshold for text under 18px. Visible in desktop-light/settings.png: "Accepts either a bare model or shorthand like claude/opus…" is markedly fainter than in the dark shot (dark computes ≈4.6:1 and passes). DESIGN.md § Do's and Don'ts states both "Do maintain WCAG AA contrast — representative pairings were tuned for both themes" **and** "`fg-overlay` is helper/disabled text only" — the canon sanctions this token for exactly the role where its light-theme value fails.
  - *Fix:* Darken light-theme `--fg-overlay` to ≈`#6b7080` (≈4.6:1 on `bg-mantle`), or restrict `--fg-overlay` to disabled/placeholder chrome in DESIGN.md and move helper text (`.form-hint`, `.section-note`, page subtitle) to `--fg-sub0`.

### `settings/global` (1)

- **HIGH** · *component-adoption* — **The canon ships no form primitives and no tab component, so the app's densest surface invented flat ones (and two divergent tab bars)**
  - `dev/design-system/components.css`
  - *Evidence:* grep for `form-input|form-label|form-select|form-group|fieldset` in `dev/design-system/components.css` returns **zero** hits; the served `design-system.css` is a byte-identical sync (diff = header comment only). Every form declaration on the ten-tab settings surface therefore lives in `app.css:817-848` and was written flat: `.form-input, .form-select` get a border and `background: var(--bg-base)` but no `box-shadow: var(--inset-sm)`, no `appearance: none`, and no chevron mask — despite DESIGN.md § Native selects prescribing "same surface, **inset depth**, accent focus ring, and a **custom DartClaw chevron** rather than the browser-default arrow chrome" (desktop-dark/settings.png crop confirms the Effort select carries the UA chevron with no divider and the input wells are flat). The same gap exists for tabs: design-system.css has no `.tab*` rule at all, and the app grew two near-duplicate implementations that diverge on mobile — `.settings-tabs/.settings-tab` (app.css:1167, sticky + clipping overflow) and `.tab-bar/.tab-btn` (app.css:1056, `flex-wrap: wrap`).
  - *Fix:* Add a Forms section to components.css: `.form-field`/`.form-label`/`.form-input`/`.form-select` (with `inset-sm`, `appearance:none` and the `--icon-chevron-down` mask already used by `.composer-model` at design-system.css:627-633) plus a field-width scale, and one `.tabs`/`.tab` component; then delete the app-local duplicates and fold `.settings-tabs` / `.tab-bar` onto it. Document both in DESIGN.md § Components.

### `shell/sidebar` (1)

- med · *color-depth* — **Every text tier in the sidebar renders at exactly one color — section labels, session titles, nav items and the archived disclosure are all `--fg-sub0`**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/design-system.css:248`
  - *Evidence:* Measured peak luminance in desktop-dark/settings.png is identical (215-scale grayscale peak = 174, i.e. `--fg-sub0` #a6adc8) for all of: "WORKSPACE" label (16,59), "CHATS" label (16,257), session title row 1 (52,341), "ARCHIVED" toggle (16,490), nav item "Health" (46,571). Sources: `.sidebar-section-label { color: var(--fg-sub0) }` (design-system.css:248-256), `.session-item { color: var(--fg-sub0); font-size: var(--text-sm) }` (design-system.css:185-201, no font-weight → 400), `.sidebar-nav-item { color: var(--fg-sub0); font-size: var(--text-sm) }` (design-system.css:257-268), `.sidebar-archive-toggle { color: var(--fg-overlay) }` (app.css:186). Sizes span only 12px→13px. So a 260×900px rail — the chrome on 100% of views — has no primary/secondary contrast at all; only the single `.active` row differs. DESIGN.md's own typography table is self-contradictory here: `body-sm` 13px is assigned to "Sidebar items" while `label-md` 14px/500 is assigned to "UI labels, session titles", and the CSS resolves the ambiguity in favour of the flattest reading.
  - *Fix:* Resolve DESIGN.md's table (session titles = `label-md` 14px/500 `--fg`; section labels stay `caption` 12px/600 `--fg-overlay`; nav items `body-sm` 13px `--fg-sub0`), then apply: raise `.session-item` / `.sidebar-nav-item` active+resting titles to `--fg`/`--fg-sub1` and drop `.sidebar-section-label` to `--fg-overlay` so three distinct tiers exist.

### `tasks` (1)

- med · *layout-spacing* — **CANON GAP: a single 900px container-max is applied to data-dense pages, which is the upstream cause of the crushed table columns**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/tokens.css:106`
  - *Evidence:* tokens.css:106 defines exactly one content width, `--container-max: 900px`, documented at DESIGN.md:411 as "Max content / message width" and justified at DESIGN.md:395 as "reading comfort". `.page-inner` applies it unconditionally (app.css:600), so the 8-column tasks table is squeezed into 900px on a 1440px viewport — visible in desktop-dark/tasks.png where content ends at x=1300 with 140px of viewport left empty while PROVIDER, CREATED and TOKENS all wrap. Reading-measure width is the correct constraint for chat and prose and the wrong one for a table, and canon offers no alternative, so faithful adoption produces the defect. Every downstream fix (nowrap headers, wider status column, un-wrapped timestamps) is fighting a 900px budget that cannot hold 8 columns.
  - *Fix:* Add a second layout token to canon — e.g. `--container-wide: 1200px` — with a rule in DESIGN.md § Layout primitives ("prose/chat use `container-max`; tables, boards and dashboards use `container-wide`"), applied via a `.page-inner--wide` modifier on the tasks, scheduling and workflow-list pages.

### `tasks + task-detail` (1)

- med · *color-depth* — **CANON GAP: light-theme card elevation is below the perceptual threshold — cards differ from the page by 2/255 and read as flat gray**
  - `dev/design-system/tokens.css:205`
  - *Evidence:* Sampled from desktop-light/task-detail.png: page background (232,235,240), meta card (230,233,239), session card (230,233,239) — a 2-unit delta, roughly 0.7% luminance. The card is therefore defined entirely by a 1px `#ccd0da` border plus `--shadow-sm: 0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,.06)` (tokens.css:206), and `--border-highlight: rgba(0,0,0,0.06)` (tokens.css:205), which in light mode is a *dark* top edge rather than the "luminous top-edge on cards" its own comment claims. The dark theme has real separation by comparison — page (29,30,43) vs card (24,24,37) with `--shadow-sm: 0 1px 3px rgba(9,9,26,0.50)` (tokens.css:123), an ~8x stronger shadow. The 7-level surface ladder (DESIGN.md:361) collapses in light mode: bg-base and bg-mantle are visually one level, so a page of stacked cards flattens into one gray field however faithfully the app adopts `.card`.
  - *Fix:* Retune the light theme in `dev/design-system/tokens.css`: separate `--bg-mantle` from `--bg-base` by a perceptible step (or invert so cards are lighter than the ground, the usual light-mode convention), strengthen `--shadow-sm`/`--shadow-md`, and make `--border-highlight` an actual light top edge in the light theme. Re-sync the served copy afterwards.


## B. Glitches — visibly broken, no design decision needed (72)

Cheap, mechanical, no design decision. Candidates for a point release.

### `tasks` (7)

- **HIGH** · *interaction-glitch* — **Tasks table breaks its column headers mid-word ("PROVIDE/R", "CREATE/D", "TO/KE/NS") and cannot scroll to escape**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/app.css:1617`
  - *Evidence:* app.css:1614-1660 sets `.task-status-group .table-wrap { overflow-x: visible }` (removing the escape hatch), `.data-table { table-layout: fixed }`, `th, td { white-space: normal; overflow-wrap: anywhere; word-break: break-word }`, then assigns eight percentage widths (34/12/10/9/11/9/10/5%). Inside the 900px content column, column 8 is 45px and column 4 is 81px — narrower than "TOKENS" and "PROVIDER" set at `--text-xs` (12px) with `--tracking-caps` (+0.08em). Result, visible in both header rows of desktop-dark/tasks.png and desktop-light/tasks.png: "PROVIDER"→"PROVIDE / R", "CREATED"→"CREATE / D", "TOKENS"→"TO / KE / NS", and the cell value "129d ago" wrapping to two lines. The wrapped headers inflate the header row to ~60px and leave a large empty band under it.
  - *Fix:* In app.css:1622-1627 scope the wrapping to `td` only and set `.task-status-group .data-table th { white-space: nowrap; overflow-wrap: normal; word-break: normal }`. Restore `overflow-x: auto` on `.table-wrap` and give the table a `min-width` (~820px) so narrow columns scroll instead of shattering. Longer term, drop `table-layout: fixed` and let the eight columns size intrinsically.

- **HIGH** · *layout-spacing* — **Task table headers break mid-word at desktop width ("PROVID/ER", "CREATE/D", "TO/KE/NS") because word-break:break-word is applied to <th> under table-layout:fixed percentage columns**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/app.css:1620`
  - *Evidence:* app.css:1617-1628 sets `.task-status-group .data-table { table-layout: fixed }` and then applies `white-space: normal; overflow-wrap: anywhere; word-break: break-word` to BOTH `th` and `td`. Columns are fixed percentages (app.css:1629-1660): 34/12/10/9/11/9/10/5%. On the 1440px capture the content column is ~900px, so TOKENS gets ~45px while "TOKENS" at `--text-xs` uppercase with `letter-spacing: var(--tracking-caps)` needs ~55px — it shatters. Visible in shots/desktop-dark/tasks.png and shots/desktop-light/tasks.png: the Review and Accepted table headers read "PROVID ER", "CREATE D", "TO KE NS" stacked over three lines, inflating the header row to ~3x its designed height on the app's busiest page.
  - *Fix:* Scope the breaking to cells only — change the selector at app.css:1622 to `.task-status-group .data-table td` and add `.task-status-group .data-table th { white-space: nowrap }`. If the resulting header widths overflow the fixed percentages, widen the tokens/created columns or drop `table-layout: fixed` in favour of `min-width` per column.

- **HIGH** · *typography* — **Table headers break mid-word ("PROVID/ER", "CREATE/D", "TO/KE/NS") because th cells inherit overflow-wrap:anywhere under table-layout:fixed with 5–9% column widths**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/app.css:1622`
  - *Evidence:* desktop-dark/tasks.png y≈505–575: the header row is 3 text lines tall; "PROVIDER" renders as PROVID/ER, "CREATED BY" as CREATED/BY, "CREATED" as CREATE/D, "TOKENS" as TO/KE/NS. Cause is app.css:1622-1628 `.task-status-group .data-table th, td { white-space: normal; overflow-wrap: anywhere; word-break: break-word; }` — the `white-space: nowrap` escape at app.css:1661-1666 is scoped to `td:nth-child(3|4|7|8)` only, never to `th`. With `table-layout: fixed` (app.css:1620) and `th:nth-child(8) { width: 5% }` (app.css:1657-1659), the TOKENS column is ~45px inside a 900px container while the label needs ~49px of glyphs (6 mono chars at 12px + 0.08em tracking) plus 24px of sp-3 padding — wrapping is arithmetically guaranteed. The same rule block is also structurally wrong: widths are keyed to `:nth-child()` while column 2 (Project) is conditional (tasks.html:71 `tl:if="${showProjectColumn}"`), so with the project column hidden every column from Type onward silently inherits the width intended for its left neighbour — even though semantic `.task-col-*` classes already exist on every cell.
  - *Fix:* Add `.task-status-group .data-table th { white-space: nowrap; overflow-wrap: normal; word-break: normal; }`; re-key all width rules from `:nth-child(n)` to the existing `.task-col-title/-project/-type/-provider/-created-by/-created/-status/-tokens` classes and give `.task-col-tokens`/`.task-col-status` enough width for their labels (≥9%).

- **HIGH** · *typography* — **tracking-caps on uppercase table headers overflows narrow columns, wrapping single words into 2-3 lines at 1.6 leading**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/app.css:1621`
  - *Evidence:* `.data-table th` (design-system.css:1578-1585) is --text-xs 12px, uppercase, `letter-spacing: var(--tracking-caps)` = 0.08em, with no line-height (so it inherits 1.6). `.task-status-group .data-table` sets `table-layout: fixed` and `.task-status-group .data-table th { white-space: normal; overflow-wrap: anywhere }` (app.css:1614-1624). At 12px JetBrains Mono the advance is 7.2px; tracking-caps adds 0.96px/char = +13.3% width, so "PROVIDER" needs 65.3px instead of 57.6px and "CREATED" needs 57.1px instead of 50.4px. In shots/desktop-dark/tasks.png the available column widths are ~69px and ~56px, so the headers break to "PROVID / ER", "CREATE / D" and "TO / KE / NS" — and at 1.6 leading the three-line TOKENS header inflates the header row to ~58px tall against 12px of actual text. Both tables on the page are affected.
  - *Fix:* Add `line-height: var(--leading-tight)` and `white-space: nowrap` to `.data-table th`, and remove the `.task-status-group .data-table th { white-space: normal; overflow-wrap: anywhere }` override (app.css:1621-1624) — let the header sit on one line and let the fixed layout size columns from it. If a column genuinely cannot fit, shorten the label ("CREATED BY" → "BY") rather than wrapping it.

- **HIGH** · *typography* — **Tasks table headers break mid-word ("PROVID/ER", "CREATE/D", "TO/KE/NS")**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/app.css:1622`
  - *Evidence:* Reproduced in both shots/desktop-dark/tasks.png and shots/desktop-light/tasks.png, on both the Review and Accepted tables. Cause is static/app.css:1622-1627, which applies the break rules to headers as well as cells: `.task-status-group .data-table th, .task-status-group .data-table td { white-space: normal; overflow-wrap: anywhere; word-break: break-word; }`. Under `table-layout: fixed` (app.css:1617-1621) the Provider column is pinned to 9% and Tokens to 5% (app.css:1641-1660); at a ~900px content width that is ~57px and ~30px of usable box, while 'PROVIDER' at `--text-xs` mono plus `letter-spacing: var(--tracking-caps)` (design-system.css:1580-1586) measures ~68px — so it wraps, and `overflow-wrap: anywhere` lets it split inside the word. The author already carved a nowrap exemption for the same columns at app.css:1661-1665 but scoped it to `td` only, leaving `th` to shatter.
  - *Fix:* Scope the break rules to `td` and add `.task-status-group .data-table th { overflow-wrap: normal; word-break: normal; white-space: nowrap; }`; widen the Provider/Tokens columns or shorten the labels ('Tokens' → 'Tok') if the header still overflows.

- med · *layout-spacing* — **Runner state badge stretches to the full card width and reads as a disabled text input**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/app.css:2089`
  - *Evidence:* desktop-dark/tasks.png y≈316-340: "Idle" renders as a 184px-wide filled box spanning the whole "Primary (#0)" card, sampled (49,50,68) = `--bg-surface0`, indistinguishable from a disabled form field. Cause: `.agent-runner-card` is `display:flex; flex-direction:column` with default `align-items: stretch` (app.css:2089-2093), which blockifies and stretches the `display:inline-block` `.agent-state-idle` badge (app.css:2103-2107). Same applies to `-busy/-stopped/-crashed`. Those four rules also re-declare the same five properties four times instead of extending the canon `.status-badge` class the template already emits (tasks.dart:280).
  - *Fix:* Add `align-items: flex-start` to `.agent-runner-card` (or `align-self: flex-start` on the state badge), and collapse `.agent-state-*` to color-only modifiers on canon `.status-badge`.

- med · *responsive* — **Consecutive status tables on mobile have unrelated column geometry and overflow their container**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/app.css:1673`
  - *Evidence:* mobile-dark/tasks.png (and mobile-light/tasks.png): the Review table places TYPE at x=325 and STATUS at x=583; the Accepted table directly beneath places them at x=497 and x=635 — two adjacent three-column tables, no column aligned. Both also run past the wrap's right edge (row rules reach x≈768 with no right border, `accepted` pills against the cut). Cause: app.css:1673-1680 switches to `table-layout: auto` below 768px, so each `<table>` — one per status group (tasks.html:65-99) — sizes independently to its own content, while app.css:1674-1676 restores `overflow-x: auto` so the excess scrolls instead of fitting. The `.table-fade-wrap::after` scroll affordance (app.css:637-638) fades to `--bg-base`, which does not match the surface it overlays.
  - *Fix:* Keep `table-layout: fixed` on mobile with percentage widths on the three surviving columns (`.task-col-title/-type/-status`) so every status group shares one geometry and nothing overflows — or render mobile as stacked rows rather than a table.

### `channel-detail` (6)

- med · *accessibility* — **Mode picker exposes two competing controls for the same setting to assistive tech**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/templates/channel_detail.html:64`
  - *Evidence:* channel_detail.html:49-63 renders four `<button aria-pressed>` cards, and :64-71 renders a duplicate `<select class="channel-mode-select channel-mode-select-hidden" aria-label="DM access mode">` for the same `dm_access` field (same again for `group_access` at :137-159). `.channel-mode-select-hidden` (app.css:1423-1432) is the clip/1px sr-only pattern, not `display:none` and not `aria-hidden`, so the select stays in the tab order and the accessibility tree — a keyboard user tabs through four toggle buttons and then a fifth control that sets the same value. Additionally, `aria-pressed` toggle buttons model independent on/off states, not the mutually-exclusive choice this is.
  - *Fix:* Keep the `<select>` as the single accessible control and mark the cards `aria-hidden="true" tabindex="-1"` (presentational), or drop the select from the a11y tree and give the card group `role="radiogroup"` with `role="radio"` + `aria-checked` on each card.
