### `settings` (1)

- med · *state-coverage* — **A failed config load leaves Settings as a wall of 22 permanently-'Loading...' disabled inputs behind a 4-second toast**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/static/controllers/dc_settings_controller.js:733`
  - *Evidence:* settings.html ships 22 inputs with `placeholder="Loading..."` and 33 `disabled` attributes, all cleared only by `populateSettingsForm` in the success path (dc_settings_controller.js:719-735). The failure path is dc_settings_controller.js:733-735: `.catch(function (err) { showToast('error', err.message || 'Failed to load settings'); })` — nothing else. shared.js:1 sets `TOAST_DURATION = 4000`, so four seconds later the only evidence of failure is gone and the page is a permanent fake-loading state indistinguishable from a slow network, with no retry. `placeholder="Loading..."` is also not a loading treatment the design system recognises — the canon's `.skeleton` (design-system.css:1364) and `.scan-bar` (:1164) are unused on this page.
  - *Fix:* On the catch path, replace the form body with a persistent inline `banner banner-error` + Retry button (the pattern already implemented at workflow_detail.html:120-125), and swap the `placeholder="Loading..."` inputs for `.skeleton` blocks during the in-flight state.

### `settings, workflows, scheduling, channel-detail` (1)

- med · *component-adoption* — **Only 2 of 17 <select> elements get the custom-select enhancement — the rest render as native OS dropdowns**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/templates/settings.html:51`
  - *Evidence:* `initCustomSelects` only enhances `select[data-enhance="custom-select"]` (static/controllers/shared.js:209-211), and that attribute appears exactly twice in the whole template set, both on templates/tasks.html. The other 15 selects — settings.html (9, e.g. :51 `#field-agent-effort`), channel_detail.html (:64, :152, :232), workflow_list.html (:26, :98), scheduling.html (:146) — stay native. The inconsistency is directly visible across two captures: shots/desktop-dark/tasks.png shows the custom pill triggers with the token caret for 'All Statuses' / 'All Types', while shots/desktop-dark/settings.png ('Effort') and shots/desktop-dark/workflows.png ('All workflows') render the platform chevron and platform popup, in platform colours that ignore the theme entirely.
  - *Fix:* Add `data-enhance="custom-select"` to the 15 remaining selects, or invert the contract so `initCustomSelects` enhances every `select` not marked `data-enhance="native"`.

### `tasks / scheduling / memory-dashboard` (1)

- **HIGH** · *color-depth* — **Every table on tasks/scheduling/memory is a card-shaped hole: .table-wrap draws a border and radius over zero surface**
  - *Evidence:* app.css:635 — `.table-wrap { overflow-x: auto; border: var(--border); border-radius: var(--radius-lg); }` — no background, no shadow, no top highlight. Canon `.data-table` (design-system.css:1568-1591) is also surfaceless by design. Measured on desktop-dark/tasks.png at y=780: inside the Accepted table (700,780) = #1d1f2e; outside it on the ground (340,780) = #1e1e2c — identical within grain noise; the only transition is a single #313244 pixel column at x=400. Used at tasks.html:66, scheduling.html:70 and :166, memory_dashboard.html:84/181/257. The result is a radius-6 rectangle of nothing, which is why the tasks and scheduling pages read as wireframes.
  - *Fix:* Wrap the tables in `.card` (or give `.table-wrap` `background: var(--bg-mantle); box-shadow: var(--shadow-sm); border-top-color: var(--border-highlight)`), and give `.data-table thead` a `--bg-sub-base` band so the header row separates from the body.

### `tasks, task-detail` (1)

- med · *brand* — **Emoji icons in the task event stream break the monochrome mask-icon system**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/templates/task_event_display.dart:67`
  - *Evidence:* `compactEventIconChar` (templates/task_event_display.dart:67-80) returns full-colour emoji for the task dashboard preview and for SSE `task_event` payloads: '🔧' toolCalled, '📄' artifactCreated, '📦' structuredOutputFinalizerUsed, '📥' structuredOutputInlineUsed, '⚠' warning/error, '💬' pushBack, '📊' tokenUpdate. Every other icon in the app is a Lucide mask painted in `currentColor` (icons.css:179-192), which is what makes the UI read as one system in both themes; the emoji render in fixed vendor colours that ignore theme, ignore the semantic accent classes sitting right beside them (`compactEventIconClass`, same file :46-62), and are the only saturated multi-colour marks on the page. Note the sibling function `eventIconClass` (:6-20) already returns proper `.icon-*` names for the exact same event kinds, so the mask path exists.
  - *Fix:* Delete `compactEventIconChar` and have the compact renderer emit `<span class="icon icon-…">` using `eventIconClass`, matching the timeline path.

### `workflows / workflow-detail` (1)

- **HIGH** · *component-adoption* — **Canon `.run-card` and `.pipeline` ship in design-system.css but are used zero times; app.css reimplements both with far less anatomy**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/templates/workflow_list.html:39`
  - *Evidence:* design-system.css:2110-2194 defines `.run-card-step`, `.run-card--attention`, `.pipeline`, `.pipeline-step--done/--running/--failed/--blocked/--pending`, `.pipeline-node` (+ pulse-ring), `.pipeline-step-body/-name/-meta`. `grep -rn "run-card\|class=\"pipeline" templates/` matches nothing. showcase.html:751-830 shows what the canon renders: status dot, `.card-header`/`.card-body`/`.card-footer`, `.tool-indicator`, `.meter`, `.identicon`, `.status-pill`, and a continuous connector spine with per-state nodes. The app instead ships `.workflow-run-card` (app.css:2771-2818 — name + badge + one 13px meta row, no dot, no identicon, no pill, no footer) and `.workflow-pipeline`/`.workflow-step-*` (app.css:2441-2600, ~160 lines with a 12px stub connector at `.workflow-step-connector`, not a spine). That is why the run board in desktop-dark/workflows.png reads as four identical gray slabs with no live signal.
  - *Fix:* Delete the `.workflow-run-card` and `.workflow-pipeline`/`.workflow-step-connector`/`.workflow-step-icon` blocks from app.css and re-render workflow_list.html:39-59 as `.card.run-card` (status-dot + name + `.run-card-step` + `.meter` + `.card-footer` with identicon and `.status-pill`) and workflow_detail.html:91-141 as `<ol class="pipeline">` with `.pipeline-step--{state}` + `.pipeline-node`.


## D. Feature-scoped (2)

Needs new capability; not polish.

### `global` (1)

- low · *state-coverage* — **Hidden nav for unconfigured features leaves no discovery path — and the one visible unconfigured card is a dead end**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/web/sidebar_feature_visibility.dart:44`
  - *Evidence:* sidebar_feature_visibility.dart:44-51 hides Health, Memory, Tasks and Scheduling entirely unless `operationalFeaturesActive` (a configured channel, Pub/Sub, or task entry point). On a fresh install none of those hold, so four of nine system nav entries silently do not exist. Settings (always visible) has ten tabs — settings.html:14-23: Agent, Workflows, Server, Sessions, Memory, Context, Scheduling, Channels, Providers, Security — with no Container/Tasks tab, so `config.container.enabled` (the primary `_hasConfiguredTaskEntryPoints` trigger, :71) has no UI at all; the only other in-UI trigger is the task_trigger toggle at channel_detail.html:207, which lives behind a channel page that is itself unreachable until a channel is configured in dartclaw.yaml. Conversely the one unconfigured feature that does stay visible is also a dead end: desktop-dark/health.png's Pub/Sub card reads 'off / Status: Not configured / Last Pull: never / Subscriptions: 0 active' with no configure link.
  - *Fix:* Either keep hidden nav entries visible in a disabled state that links to the relevant Settings tab, or add a one-time 'Set up DartClaw' entry pointing at Settings; add a Container/Tasks settings tab so `container.enabled` is reachable from the UI; and give the Pub/Sub health card a 'Configure' link to the Channels tab.

### `kg-timeline` (1)

- low · *interaction-glitch* — **Category filter is a blind free-text box on a page whose whole organising principle is category, and near-misses land in the empty state**
  - `packages/dartclaw_server/lib/src/templates/kg_timeline.html:22`
  - *Evidence:* kg_timeline.html:22 is a plain `<input name="category">` with only a placeholder. The submitted value is passed through `normalizeKnowledgeEntity` (kg_timeline_page.dart:101 → known_systems.dart:7-11: trim, collapse whitespace, lowercase) and then matched with strict equality against `fact.entity` (kg_timeline_page.dart:124). Any spelling that is not the exact stored entity returns zero groups, and the user gets "No facts recorded in this category yet." with no indication of what the valid categories are — even though the handler has the complete set in hand (`grouped.keys`, kg_timeline_page.dart:125-128) and renders them as headings on the unfiltered view. The As-of field has the same shape: free-text ISO-8601 with the only feedback being a full page reload into a 400 banner (kg_timeline.html:34-37).
  - *Fix:* Emit a `<datalist>` of the known category names from the same map the groups are built from and bind it with `list=` on the category input; on a zero-result filtered view, list the available categories in the empty state instead of a dead end.

## Refuted during verification (19)

Kept for the record — these were proposed and killed, so they do not get re-proposed later.

- **Streaming assistant text renders as raw, unparsed markdown for the whole turn, then snaps to formatted HTML on completion**
  - *Why it died:* Code claims all check out (stream_handler.dart:61 emits htmlEscape'd text; chat.html:110-111 #streaming-content has no data-markdown while chat.html:9 does; shared.js is at controllers/shared.js:213-222 and only walks [data-markdown]; finalizeTurn at dc_chat_controller.js:345-374 refetches /messages-html then calls renderMarkdown). It dies on three other grounds. (a) It is the scheduled 'chat streaming' work for 0.24+, not a 0.22 polish gap — the remedy is building an incremental streaming markdown renderer with fence/newline boundary handling, i.e. new capability. (b) The scope label 'glitch-fix' is definitively wrong: nothing here is cheap or decision-free. (c) It is not observable in any supplied capture — all four chat-session.png captures are post-turn static state, so the claim cannot be visually confirmed.
- **Tool activity renders as a bare tool name with no target, args, result or duration — and disappears entirely on reload; the canonical `.tool-call` card family is 100% unused**
  - *Why it died:* Facts verified (stream_handler.dart:66,73 emit .tool-indicator only; .tool-call defined at design-system.css:1805-1875 and grep returns zero hits across templates/ and controllers/; messagesHtmlFragment chat.dart:88-121 has exactly four branches, no tool branch). But DESIGN.md explicitly sanctions the current usage: '`.tool-call` is the structured, timeline sibling of `.tool-indicator`: the line stays the transient/inline atom, the card is the durable conversation record.' The app's live SSE stream is precisely the transient/inline case the canon assigns to .tool-indicator, and DESIGN.md gives .tool-indicator its own component section (pending/success/error) — so the streaming rendering is canon-correct, not an adoption failure. What genuinely remains is durable tool history surviving reload, which requires persisting tool events on the message record — a backend feature, and squarely the scheduled 'tool cards' work for 0.24+. Scoped 'polish-adoption', so the canon-sanction exception does not rescue it.
- **The chat page's title renders at 14px/500 — identical to message body text — because the canon's documented `page-title` (20px/600) tier has no CSS implementation**
  - *Why it died:* The stated cause is refuted. `.page-title` IS implemented — app.css:608-614: `.page-title { margin: 0; font-size: var(--text-xl); font-weight: var(--weight-bold); line-height: var(--leading-tight); color: var(--fg); }` — and is consumed by knowledge_hub.html:9, kg_timeline.html:9 and projects.html:10. So 'grep shows no .page-title class exists in either components.css or app.css' is false, and the causal clause in the title collapses. Second, the size is an explicit canon assignment, not an omission: the DESIGN.md type table row immediately above page-title reads '| `label-md` | 14px / 500 | UI labels, session titles |'. `.topbar .session-title` is an editable `<input type="text">` (topbar.html:6-8), which is exactly a UI label control. Surviving kernel worth re-filing under a truthful premise: the same string renders 20px/600 via `.info-title` (app.css:431) on session-info — verified in shots/desktop-dark/session-info.png — versus 14px/500 in the chat topbar, and canon components.css genuinely lacks a shared `page-title` class so the app forked `.page-title` and `.info-title`.
- **Canon §Messages specifies only "border + role + content", so the entire conversation column runs on two type sizes and no identity — no metadata row, no sender identicon, no consecutive-message grouping**
  - *Why it died:* no verdict returned
- **Status pill is clipped by its own column — the "accepted" badge's right border cuts through the last glyph on every row**
  - *Why it died:* REFUTED by the screenshot it names. Cropping desktop-dark/tasks.png at the cited rows (1170,760 → 90x24, upscaled 10x) shows the `accepted` pill with a COMPLETE rounded right border and a fully-formed final 'd' — nothing is clipped and no border is missing. Same result in desktop-light/tasks.png. In mobile-dark/tasks.png the table is table-layout:auto and the pills sit at x≈635-711, nowhere near any edge. The claimed mechanism is also wrong: no `overflow: hidden` exists on `.data-table td`, `.task-status-group .table-wrap` (which is explicitly `overflow-x: visible`, app.css:1618) or `.status-badge`, so `max-width: 100%` cannot clip. What the crop actually shows is a real but far smaller defect the finding does not claim: the max-width clamp eats the pill's 8px right padding so the glyph nearly abuts the border. The stated finding — clipping, missing border, cut glyph — is falsified.
- **Sibling pages in the same surface group use different layout families and therefore different mobile gutters (12px vs 16px) and different page-title handling**
  - *Why it died:* The central claim is explicitly sanctioned by canon, and the finding quotes the sanction as its own supporting evidence while scoping itself polish-adoption rather than design-canon-change. DESIGN.md § Layout: 'The parallel app-only `.page-content` / `.page-inner` family intentionally remains until its last consumer migrates: deferred in-scope tasks, task detail, scheduling, projects, and memory dashboard pages' — projects is named as a sanctioned deferred consumer. The 12px-vs-16px gutter delta is a direct consequence of that deferred family existing (app.css:599 sp-3 horizontal vs design-system.css:1565 sp-4), not an independent defect. The residual sub-claim is factually true but not what the title asserts: projects.dart:20 `pageTopbarTemplate(title: 'Projects')` plus projects.html:10 `<h1 class="page-title">Projects</h1>` does render the word twice (visible in both projects screenshots) — but this is a systemic `.pagehead` pattern shared by knowledge_hub.html:7 and kg_timeline.html:7 (both also duplicate their topbar title), and those two at least add a `.page-subtitle` that justifies the in-page head. That belongs as its own narrow finding, not bundled under a canon-sanctioned migration note.
- **Help text is present on 11 of 31 fields; the Server and Sessions tabs have none at all**
  - *Why it died:* no verdict returned
- **Type scale has no tier above 32px, so the app's only full-page hero hardcodes 4rem**
  - *Why it died:* DIES — the causal premise is unsupported. The factual half checks out (tokens.css:73-79 tops at `--text-3xl: 2rem`; DESIGN.md:391-392 assign display/metric-value as in-page tiers; app.css:311 hardcodes 4rem). But nothing forced that escape: `--text-3xl` (32px) exists and would have served, and the brief's own hard data shows --text-3xl is used ZERO times and --text-2xl once app-wide — the app under-uses the tiers it already has rather than being constrained by a missing one. "At 24px the tier would be smaller than the 20px page title by only 4px, which is why nobody used it" is speculation about author motive, not evidence, and it ignores the 32px tier entirely. The real, provable 404 defects are already captured by the two preceding findings; this adds a canon change the evidence does not require.
- **`.well` and `.well-content` are stacked on the same element in all six sub-cards**
  - *Why it died:* DIES — no demonstrated defect, and the nesting half is explicitly sanctioned. The code facts check out (channel_detail.html:73/97/161/193/205/220 all carry `class="well well-content …"`; design-system.css orders .well at 681 and .well-content at 698, so .well-content wins padding/radius while .well's `box-shadow: var(--inset-sm)` survives). But the outcome — sp-4 padding, radius-lg, a faint inset shadow — is deterministic and produces no visible fault in desktop-dark/channel-whatsapp.png; "a tier that exists in neither spec" is a hygiene observation, not a defect. The line-220-inside-line-205 nesting is directly sanctioned by DESIGN.md:535 "**Wells nest freely.**" The one sub-claim with visual teeth — a double 1px border from `.well > .well-content` in whatsapp_pairing.html/signal_pairing.html — has no screenshot in the capture set to prove it, and app.css:655 (`.pairing-section > .well-content { display:flex; flex-direction:column; gap: var(--sp-5) }`) shows that wrapper is a deliberate flex container, not dead markup. Per the default-to-false rule, this does not survive.
- **Two parallel content-column systems ship side by side; 9 templates use the app fork, 5 use the canon, and their gutters differ**
  - *Why it died:* DIES on the explicit-sanction rule, and the census is wrong. DESIGN.md:400 states verbatim: 'Migration note — `.content-area` / `.content-inner` is canonical. The parallel app-only `.page-content` / `.page-inner` family intentionally remains until its last consumer migrates: deferred in-scope tasks, task detail, scheduling, projects, and memory dashboard pages, plus the out-of-scope knowledge UI.' The shipped state matches that sanctioned list EXACTLY — the seven fork consumers are tasks, task_detail, scheduling, projects, memory_dashboard, knowledge_hub, kg_timeline. So this is a documented, explicitly deferred debt item, not a 0.22 polish defect, and the finding is scoped polish-adoption rather than arguing the canon is wrong, so it does not qualify for the exception. The counts are also wrong: it is 7 fork / 6 canon, not 9/5, and workflow_list.html is named as a fork consumer when it actually uses `class="content-area"`. (The 12px vs 16px mobile gutter delta is real — measured first-content-x of 12 on mobile-dark/tasks.png vs 15-16 on mobile-dark/settings.png and health.png — but a 4px gutter difference on a sanctioned migration path does not explain 'under-designed'.)
- **Four different KPI value sizes across four dashboards (32 / 20 / 16px) from two near-homographic classes**
  - *Why it died:* no verdict returned
- **Three heading rules omit font-weight and inherit UA bold 700, a weight the loaded webfont does not ship — browsers synthesize faux bold**
  - *Why it died:* The mechanism is refuted. Blink decides synthetic bold in CSSSegmentedFontFace::GetFontData as `capabilities.weight.maximum < BoldThreshold() && font_description.Weight() >= BoldThreshold()`, with BoldThreshold = 600; WebKit uses the equivalent test. The Google Fonts face set here maxes at 600, so 600 < 600 is false and a 700 request resolves to the 600 face with NO synthesis. The rendered claim therefore also fails: I cropped and upscaled both 'README' (16px) and 'Knowledge Hub' (20px) from knowledge-hub.png and they show the same stem-to-height ratio with none of the doubled-stroke smearing synthetic bold produces — 'noticeably heavier-stemmed' is not visible. The underlying CSS observation is accurate (.knowledge-result-title app.css:3162-3166 on an <h2> at knowledge_hub.html:49, .channel-detail-hero-title h1 app.css:1300-1303, .channel-sub-card h3 app.css:1435-1439 all omit font-weight), but it produces no defect on the shipped font — it would only bite if JetBrains Mono failed to load and a fallback with a real 700 face took over, which is a different finding (the unvendored-font one).
- **Semantic status badges are less chromatic than the neutral surfaces they sit on — the colour lives only in 12px text**
  - *Why it died:* Dies on three counts. (1) The CSS is misquoted: design-system.css:1246-1257 is the BASE `.status-badge`; the variants immediately below (1259-1284) do tint the fill and the border — `.status-badge-success { background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--success) 10%, var(--bg-base)); border-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--success) 22%, var(--bg-surface1)) }`. "Only the text recoloured" is false. (2) The generalisation is refuted by measurement. I histogrammed the actual badge fills rather than single pixels: projects.png Error badge fill #33293a chroma 0.0330 and tasks.png info/review fill #292d42 chroma 0.0384 are both MORE chromatic than --bg-surface0 (0.0319) and --bg-base (0.0304). Only the green variants land low (#2c323a = 0.0166) — and that is an oklab artefact: mixing 10% green (hue 143°) into a blue-violet base (hue 284°) partially cancels the chroma vector while still shifting hue from 284° to 256°, which is exactly what makes it read as green. Chroma magnitude is the wrong metric here; it ignores hue separation. (3) Even if the numbers held, DESIGN.md § Status indicators explicitly sanctions the treatment: "Status badges — subtle semantic chip with embedded dot. Low visual weight, slightly tinted background, thin border so they still read in light mode." The finding is scoped polish-adoption but its fix (raise to ≥20% mix) would rewrite that sanction, i.e. it is a canon change argued as an adoption gap. The one surviving fact — `.status-pill` has 0 uses across 26 templates against 37 `.status-badge` uses — is true but is a bare unused-component observation, not a demonstrated defect.
- **The chart ramp and the entire extended palette are dead tokens — no consumer anywhere in the served CSS, and the health dashboard paints 3 of its 4 KPIs the same blue**
  - *Why it died:* The facts check out but the defect does not. Verified: --chart-1..6 have 0 consumers in app.css and design-system.css; --mauve/--sky/--pink/--lavender/--accent-dim are 0 in app.css, --teal is 3; health_dashboard.dart:144-149 is verbatim accent/info/info/info; and desktop-dark/memory.png does run green/blue/blue/red/blue. But the fix inverts DESIGN.md rather than adopting it. § Colors: "Extended palette — mauve, teal, sky, pink, lavender ... They are decorative/categorical only: multi-hue gradients ..., the ambient body glows, identicons, and data-viz category colors" and Don'ts: "Don't let extended-palette hues carry state or fill solid UI surfaces." The chart-ramp mandate the finding quotes — "Assign by series index, never by hand-picking" — is scoped to charts/series, and the corresponding Don't reads "Don't hand-pick chart colors ... so charts look related across views." A dashboard KPI card is not a chart series: DESIGN.md's own container flowchart routes "Is a numeric KPI?" to `.card .card-metric--{color}`, whose only defined variants (design-system.css:993-1016) are accent/info/error/warning — semantic by construction. Painting Sessions/DB Size/Task Artifacts with mauve/teal/pink would require inventing new variants and would breach "a user must never have to ask whether purple means failure." The remaining observation — a chart ramp with no consumers — is not a polish defect: the app ships no charts, so this is an absent feature, and the finding is scoped polish-adoption on that basis, which is wrong twice over.
- **The gradient divider and gradient-heading vocabulary is canon-only — section boundaries across the app are unmarked**
  - *Why it died:* The counts are right and the conclusion is wrong. Verified: .divider-fade, .divider-center, .card-header-gradient and .text-gradient all have 0 occurrences across templates/. But the title's falsifiable claim — "section boundaries across the app are unmarked" — is false by the finding's own evidence: they are marked by .section-label (design-system.css:1534-1541), which is canon's designated section marker, applied correctly and consistently (SERVICES / METRICS / GUARD ACTIVITY on health.png, OVERVIEW / MEMORY PRUNING / SEARCH & INDEX on memory.png). "Marked with the canonical marker rather than also getting an optional decorative rule" is a taste preference, not a defect. The proposed fix also collides with an explicit DESIGN.md Don't: "Don't use the accent decoratively (no green icons, no accent dividers between unrelated sections)" — and .divider-fade is defined as accent→transparent. Finally the cited consequence is a non-sequitur: I confirmed the ~260px empty region below the last result on desktop-dark/knowledge-hub.png, but that is an empty-results-set layout issue and no divider above a section label would change it. Nothing here is falsifiable as a defect.
- **Canonical .tool-call family (11 classes) is 100% unused — agent tool activity renders only as transient .tool-indicator one-liners and vanishes on reload**
  - *Why it died:* Facts check out (0 refs to .tool-call*; stream_handler.dart:66,73 emits only .tool-indicator; chat.html:1-11 persisted fragments carry no tool markup), but the finding is out-of-scope by the audit's own exclusion rule: the fix requires persisting tool events in the message stream — i.e. chat tool cards + a durable conversation record. dev/state/ROADMAP.md schedules that as '0.24 — Chat & Session Experience ... Best-in-class Web chat + session-management control-plane on the Afterglow system'. This is scheduled feature work dressed as a 0.22 polish gap, not a 0.22 adoption defect. (The narrow salvageable sub-claim — re-expressing .msg-guard-block (app.css:444-472) as .tool-call--blocked — is not what the finding is titled or scoped around.)
- **Two parallel page-layout families still ship: 7 pages on non-canonical .page-content/.page-inner vs 6 on canonical .content-area/.content-inner, with different padding and gap rhythm**
  - *Why it died:* Explicitly sanctioned by the canon, and the finding is scoped polish-adoption rather than design-canon-change, so it dies by the stated rule. DESIGN.md:400 reads verbatim: 'Migration note — .content-area / .content-inner is canonical. The parallel app-only .page-content / .page-inner family intentionally remains until its last consumer migrates: deferred in-scope tasks, task detail, scheduling, projects, and memory dashboard pages, plus the out-of-scope knowledge UI.' The sanctioned consumer list matches the finding's 7 pages one-for-one (tasks, task_detail, scheduling, projects, memory_dashboard, knowledge_hub, kg_timeline). The finding itself concedes 'DESIGN.md:400 documents the fork as deliberate-but-deferred' and then reports it anyway. The sub-claim that the two families are non-equivalent (app.css:598-600 gives .page-content asymmetric sp-6/sp-6/sp-10 and .page-inner a flex gap of sp-6) is factually correct but is the known cost of the deliberate deferral, and no screenshot pair was shown to demonstrate a visible rhythm break.
- **/health-dashboard/audit serves a naked, unstyled HTML fragment on direct navigation — no layout, no CSS, no theme**
  - *Why it died:* The screenshot is real (white/serif/native buttons), but both load-bearing claims are refuted. (a) The claimed user paths do not exist: audit_table.dart:86-91 and :156-179 emit `<button class="filter-btn" ... hx-get=...>` and `<button class="btn btn-ghost btn-sm" hx-get=...>` — plain `<button>` elements, not anchors, so they cannot be middle-clicked or bookmarked; and no site sets `hx-push-url` for this endpoint, so the URL never enters history and cannot be refreshed into. health_dashboard.html's only hx-get is a self-poll on `/health-dashboard` (line 9); the audit table is server-rendered inline at line 40. (b) The claim 'unlike every other route which goes through web_utils.dart:9 and wraps non-HX responses in the layout' is false — web_routes.dart:528 returns `htmlFragment(fragment)` for /memory/refresh with no HX-Request check at all, identical behaviour. `wantsFragment` is used by exactly 4 call sites (web_routes.dart:294, whatsapp_pairing_routes.dart:32/:132, signal_pairing_routes.dart:77/:103). This is an internal HTMX fragment endpoint behaving like every other fragment endpoint; the screenshot only exists because the capture harness typed the URL directly.
- **Show/hide is done with inline style.display in 12 places instead of the hidden attribute used elsewhere**
  - *Why it died:* DIES. It is a code-style preference dressed as a defect, resting on a fabricated project standard and a false rationale. (a) The 'the 0.22 ceiling of ≤5 justified inline styles' does not exist — no such rule appears in DESIGN.md, dev/guidelines/*.md, or any test under packages/dartclaw_server/test/static/ (app_js_test.dart, design_system_icons_sync_test.dart, embedded_static_routes_test.dart, workflow_controller_test.dart). The finding measures the code against a threshold it invented. (b) The stated harm — 'leaves no accessible state' — is wrong: an element hidden by inline display:none is removed from the accessibility tree exactly as `hidden` is; the two are equivalent to assistive tech. (c) The count is wrong: grep finds 14 style.display sites, not 12. (d) No defect is demonstrated. I checked the one way this could actually break — an element carrying both a `hidden` attribute and a style.display='' restore — and it does not occur: task_detail.html:221-222 ships the state solely as tl:attr style=${progressSectionStyle} and dc_tasks_controller.js:228 restores it with style.display='', consistently; audit_table.dart:111 pairs with dc_shell_controller.js:421-422 the same way. Nothing is broken, so it fails the glitch-fix bar, and it produces no visual or interaction difference, so it is not polish-adoption either.


---

# Appendix — milestone sequencing analysis

*Answers: should `0.next-ui-ux-improvements` be pulled before 0.24, or integrated into it?*

## Recommendation: (c) — a small polish release before 0.24, scoped as design-system canon work, **not** as a slice of Cross-Surface UX

The decisive fact: almost none of what the owner is complaining about is *in* the Cross-Surface UX backlog. Pulling that milestone forward (a) or grafting its web subset into 0.24 (b) would both consume a milestone's worth of effort and leave the flat/gray pages, typography, and font-sizing untouched. Verified by grep across `audits/audit.md`, `audit-web-delta-2026-06.md`, and `backlog-ux-inventory.md`: there is no item covering type scale, visual hierarchy, surface depth, or density. The only matches for "font-size" are the 16px iOS-zoom rule (C4) and the memory-list "flat list" remark (unrelated). The backlog is a *feature/parity* backlog, not a *visual-quality* backlog — 0.22 owned visual quality, and owned it as compliance rather than refinement.

### 1. Web-UI polish vs CLI/channel/parity split

Cross-Surface UX contents after D12 (`cross-surface-ux-plan-2026-06.md` §4):

**Not web at all — leave unscheduled (no bearing on the complaint):**
- OH-1 WA/Signal `ChannelFeedbackStrategy`; OH-2 portable slash commands + `/help`; OH-13 quiet hours/batching (SQLite-persisted, D10); OH-14 sender attribution/identity/media-ack; QW-13 in-channel `/stop`.
- OH-3 `dartclaw doctor`; OH-4 shell completion; OH-5 TTY color/tables; OH-6 `--follow`. (QW-5–8 CLI trust fixes are the earlier wave.)

**Web, but feature/IA work — medium effort, not polish:**
- OH-7 settings progressive disclosure + mutability badges; OH-8 USER.md profile editor + onboarding banner; OH-9 task list/detail IA (search/sort/bulk, push-back reveal, tool-arg pretty-print); OH-11 scheduling cron builder; QW-11 canvas card links + count badges; TR-5/TR-6/TR-9/TR-13 (autonomy band, guard verdicts in timeline, run replay, cost dashboard); TR-7 comment-on-diff.

**Web, and genuinely responsive to the complaint — only two:**
- **QW-10** — design-system confirmation modal replacing the 4× `window.confirm` (`dc_projects_controller.js:192`, `dc_scheduling_controller.js:403`, `dc_shell_controller.js:346,454`) plus retiring timed-text/`display:none` reveals. This is the *entire* native-dialog complaint.
- **OH-10** — memory dashboard's 30s full-`innerHTML` poll that resets tab + scroll (B2/N8) — the most-cited "small glitch."

Everything else the owner named — flatness, typography, sizing — is **new work with no backlog home**.

### 2. Overlap with 0.24's surfaces

0.24 owns chat + session sidebar + Cmd+K + notification centre. Of the two responsive items, **neither is on a 0.24 surface**: the `window.confirm` sites are projects/scheduling/shell; the memory dashboard is its own page. The rest of the web-polish subset sits on settings, tasks, scheduling, canvas, health, knowledge, projects — all disjoint from 0.24. The only true overlaps (QW-1 thinking indicator, QW-9 context meter, OH-12 sessions, TR-4 Cmd+K, mobile composer re-validation) were **already moved into 0.24 by D12**. That carve is correct and complete; there is nothing left to integrate. Option (b) is answering a question that D12 already answered.

### 3. Sizing consequence

0.24 targets 10–14 stories *including* Phase 0, and the 2026-07-24/25 additions (settle lifecycle, live-everywhere substrate, steer/queue, persistent drafts, command catalog + skill invocation) already pushed it over — the brief itself names a split-off order (notification centre → incognito/export → drafts). Adding the disjoint web-polish subset (OH-7/8/9/10/11 + QW-10/11) is roughly +6–8 stories: a 16–22-story milestone. To hold 10–14 you would cut Phase C entirely (Cmd+K + notification centre + keyboard layer) — i.e. trade the flagship's differentiating half for polish on pages the flagship doesn't touch. That is a bad trade, and it fragments Cross-Surface UX's remaining scope into an incoherent channel/CLI rump.

### 4. Options

- **(a) Whole milestone first** — largest cost, and ~60% of it (channel + CLI) is irrelevant to the complaint. Also inverts the deliberate D1 sequencing (TR-5/TR-6 want OH-9 and the 0.25 approval surfaces). Reject.
- **(b) Integrate web subset into 0.24** — busts sizing, forces cutting Phase C, and still doesn't fix typography/flatness. Reject.
- **(c) Small polish release (0.22.1) before 0.24** — ✅. Scope: a **canonical design-system refinement pass** (type scale/tracking, surface + elevation ladder to kill the gray flatness, density/spacing) re-synced into the app via the 0.22 drift check; **QW-10** confirm/prompt modal; **OH-10** memory polling; the observed glitches. All cheap CSS/template work, zero backend dependency, disjoint from 0.24's surfaces. Leaves both Cross-Surface UX and 0.24 scopes intact.

The strongest argument for (c) is compounding: 0.24 built on an unpolished substrate ships a polished chat surface surrounded by unpolished everything-else, and every later surface (workflow studio, desktop app) inherits the same base.

### 5. Dependencies that would make this wrong

1. **Canon-first is mandatory.** 0.22's drift check requires `design-system.css` byte-identical to `dev/design-system/`. Typography/depth fixes must land in canon and re-sync — app-side edits will fail CI. If the flatness is a *canon* deficiency rather than an adoption gap, this is a design-system revision, not a patch release, and should be scoped accordingly.
2. **QW-10 is blocked upstream.** DESIGN.md currently *sanctions* `window.confirm`; 0.22 deferred replacement explicitly for that reason. The decision-table change must land first.
3. **0.24 Phase 0 components already live in canon on `feat/0.22`.** A type-scale/spacing change ripples into `.composer`, `.tool-call`, `.approval-card`, `.notif-item`, `.palette-item`. Doing it *before* 0.24 planning avoids rework — doing it after means reworking shipped chat components. This strengthens (c) and further weakens (b).
4. **If these are 0.22 regressions, not gaps** — `feat/0.22` already carries two "Fix 0.22 UI regressions" commits and uncommitted `app.css` / `dc_settings_controller.js` changes. Anything that is a regression against the 0.22 acceptance criteria (US01 "good" on every page, US03 feedback primitives) is a release blocker for `v0.22.0`, not point-release scope. Triage that split first; it may shrink 0.22.1 to a genuine refinement pass.
5. **If 0.23 (backend) runs in parallel anyway**, 0.22.1 costs no calendar time on the UI track at all — verify the two tracks are actually parallel before assuming a schedule hit.
